FOREWORD 

This report is a compilation of information on the laws regarding veterans' benefits of the several States and the District of Columbia. 

It brings up to date and supplements the 1967 compilation of "State Veterans' Laws," published by the Committee on Veterans' Affairs as 

House Committee Print No. 99, 90th Congress, first session.

This report contains an analysis of existing State legislation affecting veterans' benefits and includes enactments of the 1968 session of the various State legislatures. Section B lists those States which have authorized the payment of a bonus to veterans of World War II, the Korean conflict, or the Vietnam era. Section C shows amounts spent for veterans' benefits in calendar year 1967. 

The committee expresses its appreciation to the Office of the General Counsel of the Veterans' Administration for preparing this important document.

OLIN E. TEAGUE, Chairman, Committee on Veterans' Affairs.

Résumé Recording of discharges.-Honorable discharges are to be recorded free of charge. 

Service officers, etc.-Adjutant General authorized to assist all persons residing in Alabama or other States, having claims against Alabama or the United States for compensation, pension, bounty, back pay, medals, etc., where such claims have arisen out of, or by reason of, service in any of the wars or insurrections concerning the United 

States or the State of Alabama.

Duties of the country and district service commissioners to aid veterans, their relatives, beneficiaries and dependents to obtain benefits to which they are entitled under Federal, State, or local laws.

Duties of State service commissioner to aid veterans, their relatives, beneficiaries and dependents to secure any compensation, hospitalization, insurance, education, employment, loan guarantees, or any other benefit to which they may be entitled under Federal, State, or local law.

State department of veterans' affairs shall assist veterans in presenting claims against the United States or the State of Alabama when veterans request assistance.

State department of veterans' affairs to inform veterans and their dependents of educational benefits available.

Employment preferences, privileges, etc. discharge, whichever is later, provided they are not receiving similar or greater educational benefits from the Federal Government. 

Deceased veterans.-Free tuition in State institution of higher learning, college, or university, for 4 years, or in any State trade school for prescribed course, to the children and unremarried widows (having children) of veterans (1) killed in action or who died in line of duty or who were listed as missing in World War I, World War II, or the Korean conflict; or (2) who died from a disability incurred from military service during such periods.

Free tuition in State institution of higher learning, college or university for 2 years, or one-half the tuition for prescribed course for State trade school, to unremarried widow of veteran killed, declared missing, or who died in line of duty or who died as a result of wartime service-connected disability during World War I, World War 

II, or the Korean conflict.

Disabled veterans.-Free tuition in State institution of higher learning, college or university, for years, or in State trade school for prescribed course to children of veteran with 40 percent service-connected disabilities resulting from service in World War I, World 

War II, or Korean conflict.

Free tuition in State institution of higher learning, college or university for 2 years, or one-half tuition for prescribed course in State trade school, to widow of veteran with 40 percent service-connected disabilities resulting from service in World War I, World War II, or Korean conflict, providing she is not receiving equal or greater educational benefits from the Federal Government. 

Scholarship fund of $1,250 donated by the American Legion or American Legion Auxiliary for scholarship to be established in certain State institutions-acceptance and administration of fund. 

Leaves of absence for State employees in Armed Forces; restoration to former positions; preservation of rights and benefits.

Leaves of absence for teachers entering the military service of the United States; effect on continuing service status. 

No vacancies in State, county, or municipal offices by reason of absence of incumbents in active military service in time of war or national emergency; preservation of rights and privileges as to reinstatement.

Preference in State civil service examinations to veterans, their wives or widows. In case of reduction in service, they shall be the last to be dismissed.

State, county, and district service commissioners shall be veterans.

Veterans not disabled. Exemption of veterans from payment of business or occupational license taxes up to $35, for period of 6 years after 

Oct. 13, 1947, or after discharge, whichever is later. Includes veterans of World War II or of any conflict in which the United States is engaged in hostilities with a foreign state. A veteran whose property, both real and personal, is valued at $7,000 or more, and whose net annual income is $3,000 or more shall be granted an exemption only to the amount of $15. 

Cigarette tax.-Tobacco products for use of members of the armed services are exempted from tax. 

Game license.--Confederate soldiers are exempt from hunting license.

Income tax. Exemption of any amount received through the War Risk 

Insurance Act or any law for the benefit or relief of injured or disabled members of the "military or naval forces of the United States." 

Military compensation paid to a person in time of war is exempt from income tax.

Motor vehicles.-Disabled veteran of World War II or of any other hostilities in which the United States was, is, or will be engaged against a foreign state, who receives a motor vehicle from the Administrator of Veterans' Affairs is exempt from payment of license fees and ad valorem taxes if the automobile is operated only for the veteran's personal use. 

Disabled veterans requiring special mechanical control devices to operate an automobile are entitled to a special motor vehicle license tag. A license fee of $1 must be paid and is in addition to the tax which is assessed against such automobile. 

Peddler's license.-Confederate soldiers are exempt from fee. 

Property tax. Property of all incompetent veterans to the value of $3,000 shall be exempt from ad valorem taxation. 

Property tax. The home of any veteran which is or was acquired by him pursuant to the provisions of Public Law 702, 80th Cong., as amended, (38 U.S. C.A., ch. 21, § 801: Paraplegic), regardless of its value, shall be exempt from all ad valorem taxation so long as the same is owned and occupied as a home by such veteran or his unremarried widow. 

Veterans of Foreign Wars, or American Legion: All property owned by these organizations or any post thereof, is exempt from ad valorem taxation provided same is used and occupied exclusively by said organization.

ALASKA 

Citations are to the Alaska Statutes, 1962, as amended World War II.-A bonus was payable in dollars equal to the number of months which the veteran spent in service multiplied by 10, but such bonus is not to be paid for service after Nov. 1, 1945. 

Limitations.--No bonus may be paid after July 1, 1964, and no bonus payable to a person who received a loan under the chapter authorizing loans to Alaska veterans.

Records of all bona fide residents of Alaska who served in World War I shall be compiled and maintained by the registrar of vital statistics.

Alaska World War II Veterans' Board and Commissioner of Veterans'

The Department of Commerce may financially assist veterans' organizations in training and employing full-time service officers to perform work for veterans, including the prosecution of their claims and solution of their problems arising out of military service. 

Public employees of State.-Preference shall be given to veterans of 

World War I, World War II, and of the Korean and Vietnam con flicts in appointment and employment.

Reemployment benefits granted by section 9 of the Universal Military Training and Service Act shall be granted to veterans who had been in the employ of Alaska or any subdivision thereof.

Korean and Vietnam conflicts. All provisions of Alaska World War II 

Veterans' Act, except those relating to payment of bonuses, are extended to veterans of the Korean conflict (i.e. for the period between June 25, 1950, and Jan. 31, 1955) and to those who served other than dishonorably on active duty between Aug. 4, 1964, and 

6 months after the termination of hostilities involving forces of the United States. Eligibility includes unmarried dependents of such veterans. To be eligible the veteran must have been a resident of 

Alaska 1 year before entry into service and returned there thereafter, or who lived in the State for at least 10 years following release from the service. No person, if he is eligible for veterans' benefits under laws of any other State or territory is eligible for these benefits, unless he lived in Alaska 10 years after release from the service. 

A World War II veteran who received a bonus as such need not repay it in order to qualify under the loan provisions of this section. 

Estates.-The Administrator of Veterans' Affairs is to be made a party in interest and to be furnished with copies of accountings when an estate to be administered by a guardian includes assets derived from benefits paid by the Veterans' Administration. 

Provisions of Alaska State Housing Authority Act to provide low-cost housing and housing projects made available to veterans of World 

War II, and their spouses and widows.

Preference given to 50 percent of the units in a housing project for rent or sale to veterans.

Public land.--Veterans of World War I, World War II, and Korean conflict given priority to purchase unoccupied public residential land. § 26.10.040. § 39.25.150 (23) as amended 1967. § 26.10.060. § 26.15.160 as amended 1966. § 20.05.170 and § 20.05.190.

Veterans' organizations. 

ALASKA-Continued 

Résumé 

Eligibility.-Persons who served in World War I or World War II, or in any period of armed conflict in a combat zone. The person must have been a resident of Alaska 1 year before induction, and returned to that State after discharge, or have lived in Alaska for 

10 years after release from service.

The Department of Commerce (formerly the Commissioner of Veterans' Affairs) may guarantee up to 90 percent, loans obtained by qualified veterans; the Commissioner of Commerce may guarantee not to exceed 90 percent of the amount loaned by a private lender, or may make, where private financing is not available, loans for (a) educational, domestic, remote area family housing, and other personal purposes not exceeding $5,000; (b) farm and home loans not exceeding $22,500, providing that where such loan is for a home, it shall not be for more than 90 percent of the appraised value; and (c) business loans not exceeding $22,500, providing that said loan shall not exceed 75 percent of the appraised value of the collateral offered as security. An applicant is eligible for more than 1 type of loan, but the total may not exceed $22,500 at any one time. 

The Department of Commerce is empowered to assist in programs for training in veterans' rehabilitation work.

Property of a nonbusiness organization composed entirely of veterans of a war of the United States, or an auxiliary of such organization is exempt from municipal taxation.

Rehabilitation of veterans.-The Department of Commerce may approve expenditures by veterans' organizations and reimburse them for employing and training full-time veterans' service officers. § 26.15.130 as amended 1968. §§ 26.15.010 and 26.15.040 as amended 1967. § 26.10.040. § 29.10.336. Citation § 26.10.040.

Income tax.-Exemption of military compensation up to $1,000, and educational benefits; extension of time for filing returns. 

Professional and occupational licenses.—Funeral directors, exemption of servicemen from license renewal fees in time of war. 

Property tax.-Exemption to resident veterans with at least 60 days' service during World War I or prior wars if have been a resident of Arizona prior to Sept. 1, 1945. Not to exceed $2,000 where total assessment does not exceed $5,000.

Exemption to resident veterans with service-connected disability as determined by U.S. Veterans' Administration if have been a resident of Arizona prior to Sept. 1, 1945, or have been a resident of Arizona at least 4 years prior to original entry into service and provided total assessment does not exceed $5,000. If service-connected disability is 60 percent or less, exemption determined by percent of disability multiplied by the assessment, but not to exceed $2,000. If service-connected disability is more than 60 percent, exemption not to exceed $2,000.

Exemption to resident veterans with non-service-connected total disability as determined by U.S. Veterans' Administration if at least 60 days' service during time of war after World War I and have been a resident of Arizona prior to Sept. 1, 1945. Not to exceed $2,000 where total assessment does not exceed $5,000. 

Exemption to resident veterans with at least 60 days' service during time of war after World War I if have been a resident of 

Arizona prior to Sept. 1, 1945. Not to exceed $2,000 for 1969, $1,500 for 1970, $1,000 for 1971, $500 for 1972, provided total assessment does not exceed $5,000. No exemption after 1972.

Persons in military service and absent from State and persons in veterans' hospitals may send affidavit of eligibility for exemption rather than appear in person before county assessor.

Recording of discharge papers required prior to application for exemption.

Wars included through the Korean-Communist campaign ending July 27, 1953.

Contributions.-Contributions to posts or organizations of war veterans may be deducted from income tax, providing no part of their net earnings inures to the benefit of any private shareholder or individual. 

Corporate rights.-Local units of federally chartered veterans' associations are exempt from requirement of publishing articles of incorporation and from payment of filing fees. 

Insignia.-Unauthorized wearing or use of insignia, badges, etc., of veterans' organizations declared a misdemeanor.

Public record certification.-Free copies of public records for wards of the U.S. Veterans' Bureau under Uniform Guardianship Act.

Recording of discharge.-Certificates of discharge to be recorded free of charge by the county recorder.

Federal and State laws.

Cosmetology Act, failure of persons in armed services to appear for examination, not a forfeiture.

Service, or while in full-time training under Selective Training and Service Act of 1940 as amended. A person so exempted becomes liable for such tax and fee 60 days after completion of such service. He shall not engage in private practice while exemption is in effect.

Veterans' organizations.

Property tax, exemption not to exceed $5,000 on home of disabled veteran who has received pecuniary assistance from U.S. Government for acquisition of home with special fixtures or facilities made necessary by disability. Exemption does not extend to more than 1 home, and is in lieu of any other property tax exemption.

Where such person sells or otherwise disposes of such property, constituting the home, and thereafter acquires, with or without assistance from the U.S. Government, any other property which he occupies habitually as a home, this exemption shall apply to the new property.

Department of California, United Spanish War Veterans, commission established, to promote for welfare and maintenance of. 

Insignia. One who sells or offers to sell or give away a poppy, badge, or label, falsely representing that it is sponsored, endorsed, or offered by a veterans' organization, or who falsely represents that a poppy, badge, or label is sponsored by a veterans' organization is guilty of a misdemeanor. 

The unauthorized wearing or use of name or insignia registered by any veterans' organization is a misdemeanor punishable by imprisonment, fine, or both. Injunctive relief or damages authorized. 

Meeting places, etc.-Cities may provide and maintain buildings, memorial halls, and meeting places for veterans' patriotic, fraternal, and benevolent associations.

Counties, cities, and towns may lease property or may acquire property and sublease it to veterans' associations, at a rental to be fixed by the municipal governing body. 

Counties may provide and maintain memorial halls, recreation centers, etc., for the use or benefit of veterans' associations. 

Memorial districts may be organized to provide and maintain assembly halls, etc., for the use of veterans' organizations. 

Use of facilities constructed for veterans' associations, may be allowed to persons or organizations other than veterans'; certain conditions.

COLORADO 

Colorado Revised Statutes

Burial. County allowance up to $50 for burial of indigent veterans. 

Burial not to be in cemetery used exclusively for burial of pauper dead. Relatives or friends may conduct the funeral.

Burial grounds.-The State is authorized to acquire and maintain burial grounds, the management and control of which is vested in certain veterans' organizations. No charge shall be made for burial space in such grounds.

Veterans, their wives, widows, and mothers who were residents of the Colorado State Veterans Center may be buried in cemetery of the center.

Headstones.-County allowance up to $20 for headstones for graves of indigent soldiers.

Acknowledgments of written instruments executed by members of the 

Armed Forces; validity.

Evidence of death or other status of persons missing in action_. 

Powers of attorney granted by persons serving in the Armed Forces of the United States, effect of death.

Public-record certification.-Free copies of public records shall be furnished when required to determine eligibility for veterans' benefits. 

Free copies of public records for wards of the Veterans' Administration, under Uniform Guardianship Act. 

Recording of discharges. Discharge papers are recorded free of charge.

Service officers, etc.-County service officers shall assist veterans or their dependents in filing claims for benefits to which they may be entitled under State and Federal laws and shall cooperate with the State department of veterans' affairs in giving assistance to veterans. 

State director of veterans' affairs shall assist veterans and their dependents in securing benefits to which they are entitled under State and Federal laws and may perform such services in this State for the Veterans' Administration as may be provided under a contractual, grant, or other basis. "Veteran" defined.. Bonus 

CONNECTICUT Résumé 

The term "veteran" as used in the General Statutes means any person honorably discharged from services in the Armed Forces. 

Definition for civil service purposes.. 

Veterans who served in Armed Forces at any time between Jan. 1, 1964, and such date hereafter to be determined by the U.S. Government as the date of termination of the Vietnam era, for a period of 90 days or more, and who at time of entering such service had been domiciled in Connecticut for at least 1 year, shall be paid $10 for each month of active service or major part thereof, rendered between foregoing dates with maximum payment of $300. If veteran dies in service while in the course of, or as a direct result of, the performance of his duty $300 shall be paid to his survivors. 

Burial.-State allowance up to $150 for burial expenses of indigent veterans; burial may not be in cemeteries used exclusively for burial of pauper dead. 

Firing salutes.-Right to have a firing squad from the National Guard, Naval Militia, State Guard, or the Organized Militia attend funeral of a veteran.

Care of graves.-Committees in cities and towns to be appointed to provide for care of graves of veterans. 

Record of graves.-Registrars of vital statistics to report to State librarian on burial of veterans.

Record of graves.-The State librarian may cause to be located and permanently identified the graves of all soldiers buried in the State. 

Headstones and markers.—Cemeteries may not prohibit the erection of a headstone provided by the State or otherwise for veterans' graves. 

Veterans' Home and Hospital Commission is required to furnish headstones, at a cost not in excess of $50, for graves of veterans. 

Veterans' Home and Hospital Commission is required to furnish upon application a metal marker and a flag holder for grave of any person who served in the military or naval forces of the United States in the Revolutionary War. Citation

Amusement tax.-Benefit performances for veterans' associations are exempt from tax.

Inheritance tax.-Exemption of war risk insurance, U.S. Government life insurance, and national service life insurance. 

Motor vehicles.-Exemption from payment of operator's license and examination fees during active military service and for 1 year following discharge.

No registration fee shall be charged a disabled veteran…. 

Refund of registration fee upon the surrender of number plates of any noncommercial motor vehicle by owner upon entering military service.

Peddler's license.-Veterans may sell goods, wares, or merchandise without a license.

Property tax.-Exemption of all property actually owned and occupied by a veterans' organization; proof of claim. 

Exemption, to the amount of $1,000, of property belonging to or held in trust for a veteran's widow, minor child, and parents; proof of claim.

Exemption, to the amount of $1,000 of property owned or held in trust for veterans of certain wars and of certain military actions and members of the Armed Forces; proof of claim. 

Exemption of property owned or held in trust for a Connecticut 

Grand Army post.

Disabled veterans.-Exemption to disabled veterans as follows: 

$1,500 with disability rating between 10 and 25 percent; $2,000 with disability rating more than 25 percent but not more than 50 percent; $2,500 with disability rating more than 50 percent but not more than 75 percent; and $3,000 in any case in which the veteran has attained 

65 years of age or the rating is more than 75 percent, or the veteran has sustained the loss of a leg or arm. If the veteran dies the property belonging to or held in trust for widow or minor children, shall be exempt in the same aggregate amount as that to which the disabled veteran was entitled at the time of his death. Proof of claim is required.

Jan. 30, 1958.]

June 25, 1950, through January 31, 1955. [Expired: Deadlines for making application was Jan. 1, 1958.]

Education and training of children of certain deceased or totally disabled veterans. 

Notary services.-No fees shall be taken for acknowledging instruments or administering oaths for persons serving in the Armed Forces of the United States, or for veterans, their widows, children, parents, or relatives, when necessary in connection with papers, etc., in support of claims for benefits.

Notary services.-Provision is made for the appointment of notaries public without cost, to such service organization as the Spanish-American War Veterans, the Veterans of Foreign Wars of the United States, the 

Disabled American War Veterans, and the American Legion. No charge shall be made for any notarial service rendered. 

Public-record certification.-Certified copies of certificates of birth, marriage, or death, shall be furnished free of charge to a veteran or his relative by the State registrar of vital statistics, and the local registrar of the city of Wilmington, when needed in the settlement of a claim. transcripts from certain records shall be furnished, upon request, by the adjutant general, properly certified, and without charge. 

Recording of discharges.-Certificates of discharge shall be recorded free of charge.

Safekeeping of papers.-Muster rolls and other records may be deposited with the adjutant general for safekeeping. 

Service officers, etc.-The adjutant general is directed to assist veterans, their families or dependents, in adjusting claims with the Government. 

The adjutant general shall designate two members of his staff as service officer and assistant service officer to assist veterans and their dependents. 

Children; resident 3 years; ages 16 to 25; veteran parents killed, died, or totally disabled in, or as a result of, World War I, World War II, Korean conflict, Vietnam conflict, or any other armed conflict of

Aides-de-camp appointed to staff of the commander in chief who have served in the Armed Forces with rank of colonel shall be appointed with such rank.

Veterans' organizations..

Bureau of war records established in office of adjutant general for preservation and safekeeping of records, relics, etc. 

Hunting, trapping, and fishing licenses.—Any member of the Armed 

Forces stationed within the State, shall be deemed a resident for purpose of obtaining license.

Income tax.-Exemption of amounts received by veterans of World War 

II under the GI bill of rights (Servicemen's Readjustment Act). 

Motor vehicles.-Motor vehicles owned and used by veterans' organizations in charitable or public welfare work shall be exempt from fee for registration. -Ambulances.-Refund of taxes on motor fuel sold and delivered and used by veterans' organizations when ambulances are used on voluntary basis.

Occupational license.-Veterans are not required to obtain license for the sale of books ("book agent") or for the occupation of auctioneer. 

Peddlers' license.-Veterans are not required to procure a license in order to engage in the business of a huckster, peddler, or auctioneer. 

Property tax. Exemption of property of the American Legion, Veterans of Foreign Wars, Polish Army Veterans of Delaware, Post 48, Inc., and property used for soldiers' restrooms.

Ambulances.-Refund of taxes on motor fuel sold and delivered and used by veterans' organizations when ambulances are used on voluntary basis.

Amusements.-Bingo game declared to be lawful when sponsored by veterans' organizations.

Budget commission is not authorized to investigate or require reports concerning burial of indigent veterans, or from the American Legion, the United Spanish War Veterans, or the Veterans of Foreign Wars. 

Insignia.-Unauthorized wearing or use of insignia, badges, etc., of certain veterans' organizations is declared a misdemeanor, punishable by a fine up to $20 and/or imprisonment up to 10 days. 

Notary services.-Provision for appointment of notaries public for veterans' organizations without cost. Appointee's authority is restricted to documents and papers in connection with and for the benefit of veterans, their families, and dependents. 

Special fee provisions.-No fee for papers required for V.A. claim.

Educational opportunities of children of veterans.

Employment preferences and privileges. 

Hospital benefits- 

Annual funds are authorized to be appropriated to aid in the education of children of veterans who died in active military service, or as the result of such service. $200 is the maximum amount allowed a child in one year for children between 16 and 21 who have been domiciled in the District for at least 5 years.

Teachers, school officials, etc.-The Board of Education is authorized to credit, as previous experience, time spent in military or naval service. 

Under the Veterans' Preference Act of 1944, appointments under the District civil service to positions in any temporary or emergency establishment shall be made in accordance with provisions of the act. 

Veterans' preference shall be given to ex-servicemen and women, their wives, unmarried widows, etc. (as specified) under District of Columbia Civil Service.

Care in VA facility.-A mentally ill person who is committed under the 

District of Columbia Hospitalization of the Mentally Ill Act and who is entitled to care and treatment in a VA facility, may be committed or transferred to a VA facility.

Transfer of Military Personnel.-A member of the Armed Forces who is arrested, apprehended, detained, or committed under the District of Columbia Hospitalization of the Mentally Ill Act shall be transferred to the custody of his service upon request of the head of his service department. 

Business license fees.-The District Commissioners may provide for waiver of payment of certain fees by persons in the military service. 

Insurance companies.-Nonprofit relief associations, composed of officers and enlisted men of the Army and Navy, are exempt from tax on insurance companies.

Income tax.-Payments to veterans or their beneficiaries, and allowances paid to veterans for disabilities resulting from military service, are excluded from gross income for tax purposes.

Motor vehicles-Operators' permits.-The Commissioners may extend, up to 6 years, the validity of a permit of a District resident on active duty with the Armed Forces or the merchant marine, who was a holder of a valid permit at time of entering the service. 

Enlisted men in the Armed Forces shall be issued a permit, without charge, to operate a Government-owned vehicle for official business.

American Veterans of World War II (AMVETS).--Property owned and used by the organization is exempt from tax. 

Veterans of Foreign Wars.-Property owned and occupied by the organization is exempt from tax.

Résumé Grave care and registration.—Municipal corporations owning a cemetery in which veterans are buried are authorized to enter into agreements with the United States for the care of the cemetery or any graves therein, and to receive funds or other assistance for the care, upkeep, and maintenance thereof.

Bodies of indigent veterans shall not be delivered to the anatomical board.

Preregistration of under-age potential voters who are in the Armed Forces or who have received orders or induction papers. 

Reregistration of Armed Forces personnel including spouse and dependents or such persons not required as a prerequisite to right to vote.

Service officers, etc.-The adjutant general is required to assist ex-service men and women with claims for compensation, hospitalization, vocational training, and any other benefits or privileges to which entitled under the law of the United States. 

Department of veterans' affairs created to provide assistance to all former, present and future members of the Armed Forces of the United States in preparing claims for all benefits or privileges to which such persons are or may become entitled under any Federal or State law or regulation by reason of service in the Armed Forces. 

The board of county commissioners of each county and the proper officers of each city may employ county or city service officers to cooperate with the department of veterans' affairs in rendering services imposed by law upon the department of veterans' affairs. 

The board of county commissioners is empowered to assist veterans and their dependents in presenting claims for benefits or privileges to which said veterans are or may become entitled under Federal or State law by reason of service in the Armed Forces. 

Architects.-Time spent in architectural activities while in military service, shall count as periods of diversified training required under 

State law.

Bail bondsmen.-Time required to obtain license after successfully passing examination is extended where delay is caused by military service. 

Dentistry.-Regulations for the practice of dentistry are not to apply to dental surgeons in the Army, Navy, Public Health Service, Coast

Guard, or Veterans' Administration. 

Embalmers (apprentice) are entitled to credit for certain practical training acquired during military service.

Teacher retirement system.-Continuation of membership in system during military service.

County or State official shall be granted leave of absence from his office to serve with military forces. 

Highway patrol.-Military service credited for retirement purposes.

Public officers and employees.--Military service shall be credited for purposes of retirement.

Résumé Beverage law.-Modification of certain requirements for veterans' organizations applying for vendor's license. 

Cigarette tax.-No tax shall be required upon cigarettes sold to any post exchange, ship service store, etc., for resale to members of the Armed 

Forces, nor upon cigarettes sold for the personal use or consumption of patients in veterans' hospitals.

Malt beverages.-Tax exemption to post exchanges and ship service stores.

Homesteads.-Veterans having a wartime service-connected disability rating of 10 percent or more are exempt from taxation on property to the extent of $500.

Method of filing claim for tax exemption by members of Armed Forces.

Service-connected paraplegic veterans and their widows exempt from real property taxation.

Service-connected disabled veterans confined to wheelchair and their widows exempt from real property taxation.

Property constituting a homestead of a person engaged in the armed services, special provisions with respect to delinquent taxes thereon.

Motor vehicle licenses.-One free motor vehicle license plate shall be issued to any veteran, resident of Florida, upon proof that: (a) vehicle was acquired through financial assistance by the Veterans' Administration specifically for purchase of an automobile, or (b) veteran has a service-connected 100-percent disability rating for compensation. License plate shall be permanent and be designated "DV". Any veteran who has been issued a free "DV" license plate may also be issued upon request a front designation plate containing only the letters "DV". 

Driver's licenses.-Honorably discharged veterans having 100 percent service-connected disability are exempted from fees for issuance of operator's or chauffeur's license. § 561.34. § 210.04(4). § 561.46(1). (Florida Constitution, art. IX, §9)

Notary public commissions.—No fee shall be charged for issuance of commission to a veteran who has 50 percent or more disability rating.

Occupational license.—Exemption of veterans employed in connection with operation of race tracks, jai alai or pelota when such veteran has a wartime service-connected disability rating of not less than 10 percent.

Veterans of any war who are unable to perform manual labor because of disability are exempt from a business or occupational license fee up to $50. Same exemption also applies to unremarried widows of such disabled veterans.

Property tax.—Property to the value of $500 owned by a person who has been disabled in war or military hostilities or by misfortune. 

Cancellation of tax sales on homes of Confederate veterans or their widows.

Discrimination against a person wearing a military uniform, is declared a misdemeanor, punishable by a fine up to $50.

Corporate rights.-The secretary of state is prohibited from issuing articles of incorporation to any veterans' organization the name of which contains the words "legion," "foreign," "Spanish,' or "dis- abled," without first obtaining approval of the national headquarters of such congressionally recognized veterans' organizations.

State division of Confederate pension and records abolished; records transferred to Department of Archives and History, function, duties, and authority transferred to State Department of Veterans' Service. 

Director of veterans' service shall maintain complete records of all Georgia veterans who served during any war in which the United 

States was engaged. 

Roster of names of Georgia soldiers, sailors, and marines who served in the War Between the States between 1861 to 1865 shall be accepted as prima facie evidence of the truth thereof.

Relief and rehabilitation County aid in food and clothing for certain Confederate veterans. 

Public records. 

Duties of director of veterans' service in assistance and rehabilitation of veterans.

Amusement tax.--Parks and places where baseball, football, etc., is played; racetracks, where various forms of racing are held, are exempt from any special tax if the proprietor or owner is a maimed or disabled Confederate soldier.

Motor vehicle licenses.-Honorary drivers licenses provided for veterans of military service during wartime, or during any conflict when military personnel were committed by the President of the United States, who are discharged therefrom under other than dishonorable conditions, for the surviving spouse of such veteran so long as she remains unmarried, and for National Guardsmen with 20 or more years creditable service therein. 

Auto tag.-Effective 1970, free auto license tag to be provided certain severely disabled veterans of military service during wartime, or during a period beginning Jan. 31, 1955, and ending on a date to be determined as a cessation of the Vietnam era. 

Fishing license. Totally disabled veterans granted permanent honorary fishing license without fee.

Homesteads.-Exemption of $10,000 from ad valorem taxation on homestead granted certain severely disabled veterans. 

Income tax.-Provision for exclusion from gross income for State income tax purposes of certain compensation received by certain military personnel for active service in a combat zone or for hospitalization for injuries or diseases incurred in such zones.

Veterans' organizations.

GEORGIA-Continued Résumé 

Auto Tag. Distinctive auto tag provided for veterans with certain disabilities, whether or not service incurred, on application and payment of fee, commencing 1968.

Peddler's license. Certain disabled veterans are exempt from payment of a peddler's license tax.

Professional and occupational licenses.-A veteran of the Spanish- 

American War, World Wars I and II, and the Korean conflict with 10 percent or more disability, and a veteran of peacetime service with 25 percent or more disability may peddle, conduct business, or practice the professions or semiprofessions without paying a license for the privilege, provided his total income is such that he is not subject to State income tax; proof of disability and nonpayment of income tax is required.

Confederate veterans may act as life and fire insurance agents or solicitors, and peddle in the State, without payment of a license tax. 

Disabled Confederate veteran may carry on business as a photographic artist, without paying a license. 

Property tax.-Determination of homestead of a person in the military service for the purpose of fixing tax exemption up to $2,000 of its value. 

Homesteads.-Veterans are to file letter from the Veterans' Administration showing eligibility for exemption from tax. 

Meeting places, camps, etc.-Counties, cities, and other political subdivisions are authorized to furnish, free of charge, a building, office, or meeting hall for the use of nationally recognized veterans' organizations.

Camps, etc.-Veterans' organizations are granted permanent easements for use, improvement, or operation of camps or campsites on State park property in Crisp County.

Specially identified automobile tag provided for commanders of veterans' organizations.

Résumé Adult education shall be furnished to veterans who are entitled to Federal educational assistance; fees shall be charged against Federal funds.

On-farm training shall be provided for veterans- Flight training assistance may be furnished by the Aeronautics Commission to Korean conflict veterans who qualify under Public Law 

550, 82d Cong.

Dentistry practiced in the discharge of official duties in the Armed Forces or with the veterans, is exempt from law regulating the practice of dentistry.

Medicine and surgery.-Commissioned medical officers in the Armed 

Forces, engaged in official duty, are exempt from law regulating practice. 

Public accountants.-A person who was serving in the Armed Forces of the United States on June 15, 1963, and who at time of entering such service met certain requirements for accountants, shall be registered upon application within 6 months after discharge.

Civil service. Credits of 5 and 10 points shall be given to veterans and their dependents in open-competitive examinations (5 percent credit to nondisabled veterans, and 10 percent credit to a disabled veteran, or his wife, or the widow of a deceased veteran).

Disabled veterans shall not be disqualified if they possess the physical capacities necessary to perform the duties of the position for which they make application.

Reemployment of public officers and employees who left positions to enter military service.

Retirement of public officers and employees.-Credit shall be given for military service during World War I.

Special benefits are provided for members of the system who enter military service on or after June 25, 1950.

Veterans' organizations-- 

Résumé 

Real property tax.-Homes of totally disabled veterans, their widows or widowers, are exempt from all property taxes. 

Property of a veterans organization used for educational, religious, community, hospital, or governmental purposes. 

Fishing.-License fees for members of the Armed Forces, their spouses, and children over 15 years of age: $2.50.

Hunting.-License fees for members of the Armed Forces, their spouses, and children shall be $5.

Medical practice.-License requirements to practice medicine and surgery shall not apply to commissioned medical officers in the Armed Forces engaged in the discharge of their official duties. 

Optometry.-Legal requirements to practice are not applicable to commissioned officers of the U.S. Army Medical Corps on duty in the State.

Osteopathy.-The board of osteopathic examiners may issue a license without examination, to a physician who passed an examination for admission into the Medical Corps of the Armed Forces. 

Veterinarians.-No license to engage in the practice of veterinary medicine shall expire while the holder is serving in the Armed Forces during a national emergency and for 6 months thereafter.

Motor vehicles.-Passenger cars furnished to disabled veterans by the 

Federal Government shall be exempt from county vehicular taxes. 

Insignia.-Unlawful wearing of badge or insignia of veterans' organization, is punishable by a fine of $20 and/or 20 days. 

Tax exemptions.-Real property of a veterans' organization used for educational, religious, community, hospital or governmental purposes, shall be exempt from tax.

Veterans' Service Agency. Abolished Council on Veterans' Affairs and functions transferred to 

Department of Social Services.

59 2269-5 "Veteran" defined_ 

Civil rights 

IDAHO 

The term "veteran" when used in laws of the State shall include any person who served in the Armed Forces during any war period officially recognized and participated in by the United States, and who has been discharged under other than dishonorable conditions. 

The term "veteran" shall include any person who served in the Armed Forces during any armed conflict or any war period officially recognized by and participated in by the U.S. Armed Forces, and who has been discharged under other than dishonorable conditions. 

Burial.-County allowance up to $75 for burial of indigent veterans and their spouses. Relatives or friends may be allowed to conduct funeral.

Acknowledgments of written instruments executed by members of the Armed Forces, their wives and dependents, validity. 

Prisoners of war.—Administration of estates of absentees including prisoners of war.

Guardians may be appointed by probate courts for minor children and estates of World War I veterans.

Absentee registration and voting for members of the Armed Forces. 

Voting residence neither gained nor lost during military service..

Notary services.-No fees to be charged by notaries public, etc., for services rendered in the matter of pension claims or claims presented to the U.S. Veterans' Administration, or Bureau of Pensions to secure benefits for veterans. 

No public officer shall charge a veteran, his widow, orphan, or legal representative, a fee for administering an oath for procuring any pension, bounty, or back pay or for administering an oath and giving the certificate required on any voucher for collection of periodical dues from the pension agent.

Public record certification.-Free copies of public records required by 

Veterans' Administration shall be furnished to applicant for such benefits.

Public official shall not accept fees for issuing copies of discharge papers or other public records (marriage, birth, death, etc., certificates) to be used in a pension claim.

Recording of discharges.-Discharge papers shall be recorded and indexed free of charge.

Résumé Service officers, etc.-County service officers may be appointed to give aid and assistance to any veteran, widow, or widower, or dependent in applying for Federal or State benefits.

Academic work in Armed Forces shall be considered as equivalent as high school work for applicants for occupational or professional licenses. No such applicant shall be denied license for failure to hold high school diploma.

Teachers.-Eligibility of veterans to teach or receive certificate to teach in the schools of the State. Certificates not to lapse during military service.

Vocational rehabilitation.-State Board for Vocational Education authorized to provide courses of vocational training to rehabilitate persons disabled in industry or otherwise.

Employment service for veterans in cooperation with the U.S. Employment Service. Preferential treatment for handicapped veterans. 

Unemployment benefits.-Retirement payments received for service in the Armed Forces eliminated from the definition of wages in computing unemployment benefits.

Idaho Veterans Affairs Commission.—Appointees must have served in the Armed Forces.

County officials, when required to be absent on account of military service shall be granted leave of absence.

Employment preference to disabled war veterans in employment in all State, county, and municipal governments and departments and all political subdivisions thereof.

Reemployment rights.-Persons who served in the Armed Forces during the period of the Korean conflict entitled to reinstatement to former position, upon making application within 90 days after relief from duty, etc.

Notice to the Veterans' Administration or other agency, bureau, or department of the United States of certain proceedings under the law on guardian and ward.

Bonus- 

ILLINOIS REVISED STATUTES, 1968 

Vietnam service.-A $100 bonus is payable for service at any time from 

Jan. 1, 1961, and prior to such time as Congress declares such persons ineligible for the Vietnam Service Medal. Eligibility requires that claimant shall have been a resident for at least 12 months before entering such service, and has received the Vietnam Service Medal. 

The widow or widower, child or children, mother, father, person standing in loco parentis, brothers and sisters, in the order named, are entitled to receive the benefit due to a deceased serviceman; and if the death is service connected and results from such service during the period above, his survivor in the order named above shall be paid $1,000. Deadline for applications is July 1, 1970.

Retailers occupational tax on cigarettes.-Portion to be applied to Service 

Recognition Bond Fund. 

Burial.-County boards are required to provide an allowance up to $250 and to appoint suitable persons to have charge of the burial of indigent veterans, their mothers, fathers, wives, widows, or minor children. Dependent relatives receiving old-age assistance not eligible hereunder.

Burial grounds.-Cemetery lots belonging to certain veterans' organizations, become the property of the municipality on dissolution of the organization. Duty of the municipality to enforce contracts for the care of lots.

Counties may acquire grounds for the burial of indigent veterans, their mothers, wives, or widows; $75 (out of county soldiers' and sailors' burial fund) shall be allowed for each such burial. 

Burial permits.-Undertakers are required to prepare certificates of burial of U.S. war veterans.

Grave registration. Registration of burial places of soldiers and sailors in accordance with public health regulations.

Résumé Grave registration.-The Illinois Veterans' Commission shall maintain a record of all veterans buried in the State. Veterans' organizations are authorized to aid in collection of data.

Headstones.—A cemetery association may not prohibit the erection of a monument or headstone prescribed or provided by the United States or the State for a veteran's grave.

Counties may provide headstones for veterans' graves.- 

The administrator of the Illinois Veterans Commission shall prepare requisitions on the Federal Government for headstones and markers, and provide for the setting up of such. 

National cemeteries may receive Federal grants for memorial gateways, etc.

Armed Forces of the United States.

Administration of estates of members of the Armed Forces listed as missing in action. Termination of trusteeship.

Oaths, affidavits, or depositions taken by, or affirmation made before, commissioned officers in the Armed Forces need not be authenticated. 

Powers of attorney granted by persons serving in the Armed Forces of the United States, effect of death.

Forces, under the Trustees' Emergency Act.

Education benefits; qualification for professions and trades.

Service officers, etc.-The administrator of the Illinois Veterans' Commission and the service officer of each field office shall assist veterans, their spouses, and widows, in obtaining benefits to which entitled. 

Vital statistics.-Certified copies to VA without charge. 

Scholarships.-Benefits under unused scholarships to the University of 

Illinois are preserved during military service.

Professions and trades.-Renewal of certificates of registration for professions and trades, after termination of military service, such as architects, barbers, beauty culturists, private detectives, horseshoers, dentists, druggists, chiropodists, optometrists, public health and registered nurses, funeral directors, plumbers, real estate brokers and salesmen, professional and structural engineers, and surveyors. 

A scholarship to a State controlled university may be awarded to any person who served in the Armed Forces during World War I, or at any time after Sept. 16, 1940, such person possessing all necessary entrance requirements. Such veterans may enroll in extension courses offered by these institutions without the payment of matriculation, tuition, fees, etc..

Illinois Soldiers' and Sailors' Children's School. The program of the school shall be to provide for the care, clothing, training, and education of children of veterans who are in indigent circumstances. The 

Department of Children and Family Services shall obtain suitable employment for children placed from the school and thereafter keep a social case record until legally discharged. Scholarships to the 

State university or normal school may be awarded to certain students; and students may attend other colleges providing similar scholarships and receive same benefits for maintenance and other expenses as those attending State institutions.

Aid up to $150 per school year provided to assist in education of each child of a veteran who died or becomes totally disabled arising from service in the Armed Forces during World Wars I and II, the Korean conflict, or beginning Feb. 1, 1955, until individuals are no longer liable for induction under the Universal Military Training and Service Act. No longer necessary [that disability must have directly resulted from performance of active military service.

Honorary scholarships.-Each county in the State is entitled to an honorary scholarship in the State university for the benefit of children of veterans of the Civil War, World Wars I and II, and the Korean conflict, preference being given to children of deceased or disabled veterans. The board of trustees of the university may, from time to time, add to the number of honorary scholarships, when such additions will not embarrass the finances of the university.

Employment preferences..

ILLINOIS-Continued Résumé 

Municipal employees.-Civil service and pension rights of municipal employees are to be preserved during military service; restoration to positions without loss of seniority.

Illinois Veterans' Commission.-Preference shall be given to veterans in the selection of members of the commission and service officers. 

Park, policemen and retirement board employees.-Preference shall be given to veterans. 

State Highway Police.-Preference in employment to veterans.

Civil Service of Cook County.-Preference shall be given to veterans.. 

Public works of the State and its local subdivisions.-Preference shall be given to veteran in employment upon public works. 

State government.-Provision for granting appropriate preference in entrance examinations to former members of the Armed Forces. 

University retirement system of Illinois.-Service with the Armed Forces is credited.

University civil service system.-Preference shall be given to veterans in appointments to positions under the system; and an additional 5 points shall be given in examinations thereunder. 

City Civil Service. Preference shall be given to veterans. Exemption from age limitations in certain positions.

Public contracts.-All loans from and contracts with RFC shall stipulate that preference in employment shall be given to ex-service men with dependents.

Chicago sanitary district.-Preference shall be given to veterans in the classified service of the district.

Courts Judges' retirement system.-Benefit rights to be preserved during military service. Citation

Municipal retirement fund.-Benefit rights to be preserved during military service.

Amusement tax, boxing, sparring, and wrestling contests.-Certain veterans' organizations holding such contests are exempt from license tax and fees.

Motor vehicles.-A disabled veteran is exempt from payment of registration fee on any vehicle awarded to him by the Federal Government, or any other vehicle which he owns as a result of trade or exchange for such vehicle. A veteran of World Wars I and II, service between 

June 25, 1950, and Jan. 31, 1955, and beginning Feb. 1, 1955, until individuals are no longer liable for induction for training or service under the Universal Military Training and Service Act, who has a service-connected disability which would have entitled him to an award of an automobile by the Federal Government under World War II criteria, or who has a total service-connected disability, is also entitled to free registration for 1 motor vehicle of the 1st division.

Also, any member of the Illinois National Guard or Naval Militia may register 1 vehicle of the 1st division for a fee of $2 annually while so serving.

Fishing.-Residents of Veterans' Administration hospitals may be issued free licenses for use of hooks and line devices. Disabled veterans also given reduced license fees for such devices. 

Property tax. All property of veterans' organizations used exclusively for charitable, patriotic, and civic purposes shall be exempt from tax. 

Property tax.-Taxes levied for 1943, 1944, 1945, or 1946, in certain counties on property belonging to members of the Armed Forces shall not become delinquent and no penalty shall be charged therefor. 

Memorial buildings in honor of soldiers and sailors of the county are exempt.

Inheritance Tax.-Exempts from inheritance tax any property or income therefrom which passes from a citizen or resident of the United States who is killed in action in a combat zone or who shall have died as a result of wounds, disease or injury suffered in line of duty in such combat zone.

Grave care and registration. Provision for care of soldiers' graves at county expense if provision for care has not otherwise been made. 

The adjutant general is required to make and preserve by counties a permanent registry of the graves of persons who served in the Armed Forces.

The soldiers relief commission in each county must secure the information requested by the adjutant general's office of every person having a service record and buried within the county. Such information shall be recorded alphabetically and by description of location in the cemetery where buried, in a book kept for that purpose.

Bonus payments to veterans of World War II and the Korean conflict are exempt from taxation.

Income tax.-The State code defines "net income" to mean adjusted 

 gross income as computed for Federal income tax purposes. 

Inheritance tax.-Property which passes to organizations composed wholly of veterans of any war, is exempt from inheritance tax. 

Motor vehicles.-Refund of registration fee for motor vehicle placed in storage by owner upon entering military service. 

Professional and occupational licenses.-Collection of fees for renewal of licenses of registered professional engineers in the military service may be deferred.

Property tax.-Property, up to certain limits, of veterans, their wives, widows, and dependents, not subject to property tax; listing (Vietnam veterans now included).

Property of associations of war veterans and of soldiers' homes exempt from tax. 

Homestead tax credit.-Statements required to be made by homesteader in order to avail himself of certain benefits may be filed by member of his family when homesteader is in military service. 

Entire tax on homestead is exempt to veteran, spouse, and minor children, if income previous 12 months was less than $5,000, providing homestead was acquired under United States Code, title 38, ch. 21, secs. 801-802. Includes Korean and Vietnam conflicts.

Special assessments.-The city council may exempt from special assessments for street improvements, etc., the homestead of a veteran of the Civil or Mexican Wars. 

Property tax. All personal property of member of Armed Forces in time of national emergency in excess of $300 shall be assessed but tax waived during such period of service.

Cosmetologists and manicurists.-Renewal certificates of registration by persons failing to renew while in the Armed Forces shall be obtainable upon filing application and paying renewal fee for the current year in which discharged.

Professional and occupational licenses may be renewed within 6 months after discharge by paying the fee required for such year (without paying back fees accrued while in service).

Land-grant colleges and universities.-Military training at universities required under Morrill Act permissive only.

Public offices or positions.-Absence caused by military service not to create forfeiture of, or vacancy in the position, other than "temporary vacancy".

Résumé

Burial.--State allowance of $250 for burial of indigent veterans or their widows. Burial may not be in Potter's field. Cities and towns may pay for the burial and be reimbursed by the Department of Veterans' 

Services. Any veteran may be buried in Maine Veterans Memorial Cemetery.

Grave care and registration.---Care of graves of veterans located in ancient or public burying grounds.

Municipalities are required to decorate veterans' graves on 

May 30 of each year.

Evidence of death or other status of persons missing in action or on active duty with the Armed Forces.

Hunting and fishing license may be issued, at the resident license fee of $4, to a member of the Armed Forces, his spouse or child (over 16 years of age), stationed at a military or naval base within the State. 

A fishing license may be issued to a Maine resident, stationed outside State, while home on leave or furlough, for $1, which is valid during leave or furlough.

Powers of attorney, deeds, leases, contracts, and other instruments executed by a person in the Armed Forces; validity. 

Motor vehicle licenses.-Operator's license issued by Armed Forces in foreign countries to members valid 45 days after licensee has returned to United States. Public Law 1967, ch. 39. Out-of-State license held by nonresident spouse who accompanies member of Armed Forces to State is valid in State.

Motor vehicles.-Examination for operator's license may be waived for persons who have been members of the Armed Forces since Dec. 7, 1941.

Amputees are exempt from payment of operator's license fee on specially equipped automobile furnished by Government. 

Amputees or blind veterans who are recipients of automobiles from the Government are exempt from payment of registration fees; specially designating plates to be issued. 

Automobiles owned by veterans who are granted free registration under 

Rev. Stat. (1964), title 29, sec. 251, are exempt from excise tax.

Automobiles sold to veterans who are granted free registration under 

Rev. Stat. (1964), title 29, sec. 251, are exempt from sales tax.

Operator's licenses may be issued to persons in the Armed Forces without payment of fee in time of war or national emergency. 

Poll tax.-Polls and estates of veterans and servicemen, their unremarried widows, or minor children are exempt from taxation.

Exemption of amputee from payment of fee for motor vehicle operator's license.

Exemption of amputee from payment of registration fee for specially designed motor vehicle. 

Corporate rights.—Procedure for incorporation of veterans' organizations. 

Insignia.-Unauthorized wearing or use of the insignia of certain veterans' organizations is punishable by a fine up to $20 and/or imprisonment up to 30 days.

Tax exemptions.-Real estate and personal property of veterans' organizations are exempt from taxation.

Amusements, Beano.-A 6-day license may be issued to veterans' organizations to operate the game of Beano.

MARYLAND 

Unless otherwise stated all citations refer to the Annotated Code of Maryland, 1957 Constitutional restriction on loans and extension of credit shall not be construed to prohibit raising of funds, upon favorable vote of the people, for the purpose of aiding or compensating war veterans. [Soldiers' bonus law (Laws 1922, ch. 448) was held unconstitutional.] 

Burial. Veterans' relief fund may be used to provide necessary funeral expenses of indigent veterans, which in no case shall be less than $100. (The "not less than $100." became effective July 1, 1966).

Burial grounds.-Cemetery near Point Lookout which was established for interment of Confederate prisoners of the Point Lookout Prison, ceded to U.S. Government for purpose of its perpetual care. 

Grave care and registration.--The Maryland Veterans' Commission shall make and preserve a permanent registry of the graves of all persons who served in the military or naval forces of the United States in time of war, and who are buried in the State of Maryland. 

Acknowledgments of legal instruments executed by persons serving in the Armed Forces of the United States and their spouses, and their dependents; validity.

Official findings.-Written finding of presumed death, missing in action, etc., by authorized individual shall be received in court as evidence of death, etc. 

Powers of attorney granted after Dec. 7, 1941, by a member of the 

Armed Forces; effect of death.

Revocation of letter testamentary or of administration or of guardianship, of an executor, administrator, or guardian, engaged in war service. Reinstatement after termination of military service. 

Soldiers' and Sailors' Civil Relief Act (expired; in force until 6 months after termination of war; war terminated Apr. 28, 1952).

Suspension of fiduciary powers during service with the Armed Forces-

Civil rights. 

Claims; assistance in obtaining benefits. 

Educational benefits; qualification for professions and trades.

MARYLAND-Continued 

Résumé Absentee voting by servicemen and their spouses.. 

Public records.-Copies furnished free of charge for wards of the Veterans' Administration, under Uniform Veterans' Guardianship Act.

The clerks of the several courts are required to furnish, without charge, copies of papers or records in their offices for use in connection with claims against the United States for benefits. 

Birth, marriage, or death certificates required by a Federal,

State, or local department or agency; of a veteran, his wife, widow, or children, shall be furnished free of charge.

The State board of health is required to furnish, without charge, copies of birth or death records for use in connection with claims against the United States for benefits.

Discharge papers.-Honorable discharge papers of persons who have served in the Armed Forces of the United States shall be recorded in separate book free of charge. Such record or certified copy shall be admissible as evidence in any court of the State. 

Service officers, etc.-The Maryland Veterans' Commission shall assist veterans or their dependents in preparing and presenting their claims to the United States for benefits to which entitled. The commission shall appoint a "State service officer" and such other employees as may be provided for in the budget.

High school certificates.-Equivalent certificate may be issued to members of the Armed Forces, 17 years of age or older, upon examination in the usual high-school subjects. 

Licenses.- Renewal of professional, trade, etc., licenses within 1 year after release from military service. Law applicable to renewals under local ordinances and regulations and public general or local laws.

Licensed real estate broker entering the Armed Forces entitled to designate individual to carry on his business, renew his license, and in case of death of veteran a member of his family may continue with the business for remainder of license or 6 months.

Apprentices.-Service in the Armed Forces is credited in connection with time required for apprentice in, or in preparation for, any profession, trade, or calling.

Children of veterans who were killed in action or who died or became permanently and totally disabled as a result of military service after Dec. 7, 1941, shall, upon application, receive aid for tuition, matriculation fees, board, room rent, books, and supplies while attending an educational or training institution of college grade or any other institution of higher learning or commercial training, a teachers college, a vocational school, or any accredited preparatory school if such beneficiary is preparing to enter the Military, Naval, or Coast Guard Academies. Such grants may be used for the matriculation fees at any of such Government academies. The maximum amount of such aid is $500 per year per child. 

Unemployment insurance.-Persons who had active service with the 

Armed Forces between Apr. 1, 1940, and Mar. 31, 1947, and are entitled to unemployment compensation under Federal law, are disqualified for benefits under State law until such Federal benefits are exhausted.

No member of the Armed Forces of the United States who has been released from active duty who is receiving either retirement, retainer, or disability benefits from the Government of the United States as a result of such active duty, and who is otherwise qualified to receive benefits under this article shall have such benefits reduced as a result of said retirement, retainer, or disability payments from the Government of the United States; and the provisions of this article shall extend to a surviving spouse of such member provided such spouse remains unmarried.

Classified service, merit system.-Disabled veterans of wars in which the United States has been engaged, including such veterans serving between June 1, 1950, and June 1, 1953, and their wives, shall be credited 10 points in examinations for appointments. Nondisabled veterans are given a credit of 5 points.

Workmen's compensation.-Wages paid in part by the United States under any Federal veterans' benefit law, included as "average weekly wages" under State workmen's compensation law. 

Maryland Veterans' Commission. -Members shall be Maryland veterans.

War Memorial Commission.-All members appointed after June 1, 1945, shall be Maryland war veterans.

Résumé County or municipal civil service or merit system law.-Rules may be promulgated to grant special credits to veterans and their wives. 

Greater credits may be allowed disabled veterans. Veterans under 

55 years of age may be exempt from any age limitation or requirement.

Police department.---Persons resigning from department to serve in the 

Armed Forces are eligible for reappointment. 

Pension and retirement funds.-Status of persons serving in the Armed 

Forces during certain specified periods.

Teachers' retirement system.-Credit allowed teachers who were members of system, absent in military service during World War I, or for period between Sept. 1, 1940, and Dec. 31, 1945, or between June 1, 1950, and June 1, 1959.

Reemployment of veterans of World War II and the Korean conflict 

(between June 1, 1950, and June 1, 1959) are entitled to reinstatement to former positions with seniority rights, etc., if application is made within 90 days after separation from service or discharge from hospital.

State employees who leave their position after Feb. 1, 1963 to enter the 

Armed Forces are entitled to reinstatement to former positions with State, with seniority rights, if application is made within 90 days after separation from service or discharge from hospital. 

State employees retirement system.-War service credit for veterans of

World War I. 

Benefits, rights, and privileges accorded veterans of World War I are extended to veterans of the Second World War and to persons with service between June 1, 1950, and June 1, 1959, to members of Reserve components, and to persons inducted in the land or naval forces of the United States.

Administration of an estate of a member of the Armed Forces dying in active service, when the assets consist only of compensation due from the United States.

Fishing license.-Convalescent patients from veterans' hospitals may obtain permit from special service officer or the Red Cross to fish without license.

Korean conflict.-Amount: $10 for not less than 90 days' domestic service, $200 for more than 6 months' domestic service, and $300 for survivors of persons who died while in active service. No deadline for filing applications.

Burial. Any employee of a city or town who is a veteran may, when authorized by the mayor or selectmen, attend the funeral or memorial services of a veteran without loss of pay. 

Duties of burial agents in cities and towns.

A permit for the burial of person known to have been a veteran will not be issued until an affidavit giving certain information has been filed with the board of health.

Sums are allowed for the burial of indigent veterans, adult dependents, wives, widows, or dependent children. Burial shall not be in a potter's grave. Relatives may conduct the funeral. 

Death certificates.--Death certificates shall set forth the military record of the decedent.

Dissection of bodies. The dead bodies of veterans may not be turned over to medical schools for dissection. 

Grave care and registration.-Cities and towns may appropriate money for the care and decoration of graves by veterans' organizations. 

Cities and towns may appropriate money to be expended for the care of veterans' graves. The duty of putting flags on veterans' graves is upon the city and town where the cemeteries are located. 

Headstones. Cities and towns may appropriate money for erecting headstones at soldiers' graves.

Résumé Free tuition for any Vietnam veteran attending a State institution for higher learning. Vietnam veterans are also eligible to take university extension courses free of charge.

Plumbing inspectors.-War service shall be credited as practical experience for the purpose of qualifying as a plumbing inspector. 

Professional and occupational licenses.-Certain licenses, permits, and certificates may be renewed within 6 months after termination of military service. No fee shall be charged for the period between the expiration and renewal dates.

Vocational education.-The State board for vocational education is directed to establish and maintain such schools, classes, or courses for training, as may be deemed advisable and necessary for the education, training, and rehabilitation of disabled veterans and other veterans of World War II. 

The division of vocational education of the department of education may establish and maintain in cooperation with local public or vocational school authorities, one or more schools for institutional on-farm training for veterans for World War II and the 

Korean emergency.

Practical art classes established and maintained by towns shall make provision for rehabilitation of disabled soldiers.

The Commonwealth may contribute toward the expenses of the higher education of children of veterans of World War II or Korca, who died as a result of military service. Amount: $750 maximum per child per year for 4 years.

Labor service.--Veterans, their widows and widowed mothers shall be given preference in appointments to the labor service of the State departments.

Retirement systems.-Credit shall be allowed for military service under the State employees' retirement system, the teachers' retirement system, and other contributory retirement systems established in counties, cities, and towns.

Club license. Exemption of veterans' organizations from law providing for the licensing of clubs dispensing food and beverages to members and guests. Not applicable to clubs where alcoholic beverages are sold.

Motor vehicles.-Disabled veterans are exempt from payment of excise tax and registration fees on motor vehicles furnished by the Federal Government and specially designed for the use of amputees. 

Peddler's license.-Disabled veterans of World Wars I and II and the Korean conflict are exempt from fees.

Insurance brokers' licenses.-Veterans are exempt from payment of fee for insurance brokers' license.

Property tax.Exemption of real property in varying amounts from $2,000 to $10,000 for stated classes of veterans and dependents fulfilling statutory requirements. 

Ticket agency license.-Veterans' organizations may sell tickets to theatrical exhibitions, public amusements, etc., with license.

Motor vehicles.-Certain disabled veterans are exempt from payment of excise tax and registration fees. This exemption applies to not more than 1 motor vehicle owned and registered for the personal, noncommercial use of such veteran. 

Parking privileges. No penalty for illegal parking shall be imposed upon any disabled veteran whose motor vehicle bears the distinctive number plates authorized by law. 

Special tags. Number plates issued to disabled veterans who own and operate cars specially designed for amputees shall be of a distinctive type.

Alcoholic beverages.--Special license permitting the sale of alcoholic beverages to members and guests only may be issued to any veterans' organization which owns, operates, etc., a building for the accommodation of its members.

Blood donations.- A person sentenced to imprisonment for 30 or more days may have his sentence reduced by 5 days for each pint of blood donated.

Conventions, reports of proceedings.-State publication of the proceedings of veterans' organizations; provision for distribution to organizations and to libraries.

Corporate rights, etc.-Certain veterans' organizations are exempt from the law relating to registration of charitable corporations and the making of annual reports. 

Disabled American Veterans' Hospital Day (1st Sunday in December) observance.

World War I Hospital Day (1st Sunday in April). 

Insignia; uniforms.-Penalties are imposed for discrimination against military personnel; and for unlawful wearing of the military uniform or any distinctive part thereof. 

The unauthorized wearing or use of the insignia, badges, etc., of certain veterans' organizations is punishable by a fine of up to $20 and/or imprisonment up to 1 month. 

Meeting places, etc.--Armory privileges. 

Cities and towns may provide headquarters for veterans' organizations. 

Room 27 in the State house, formerly used by the Grand Army of the Republic and now dedicated as a shrine to that organization, may be used as a meeting place for organizations formerly affiliated with the Grand Army of the Republic. 

Rooms in State house for the several departments of war veterans. 

Parades, flags, etc.-Drill and parade privileges. 

Names of organizations may not be inscribed on the flag of the United States, but inscriptions are permitted on streamers attached to the flagstaffs.

By ch. 716 of the Acts of 1966, a Vietnam veteran was described as one who had served more than 180 days on active duty in the Armed 

Forces of the United States between Feb. 1, 1955, and the termination of the Vietnam campaign, as declared by proper Federal authority. 

This act will result in Vietnam veterans receiving many of the privi leges of World War I and II and Korean veterans.

 MICHIGAN 

Michigan Statutes Annotated, 1930 edition

Résumé World War II.-$10 for each month of domestic service, and $15 for each month of foreign service; maximum $500. Survivors of persons who died as a result of military service may receive difference between what was received and $500. (Deadline date for filing was Dec. 31, 1954, except for veterans still in service or in a veteran's or State hospital, the date was June 1, 1956.)

Korean conflict.-$10 for each month of domestic service, and $15 for each month of foreign service; maximum $500. Survivors of persons who died as a result of military service may receive difference between what was received and $500. (Deadline date for filing was 

Mar. 7, 1958.)

Burial.-County allowance up to $300 may be provided for veterans, their wives or widows, whose estates do not exceed $15,000 including veterans of Vietnam conflict.

Remains of war veterans may be disinterred for removal to soldiers' burial grounds.

Powers of the Grand Army of the Republic, Sons of Veterans, Military Order of the Loyal Legion, World War Veterans, and United 

Spanish War Veterans, as corporations to establish cemeteries. 

Headstones and markers -Municipalities may furnish, at public expense, metal markers for soldiers' graves, to mark graves for memorial purposes. 

Destroying, defacing, or carrying away without authority any marker, or memorial flag placed at the grave of any veteran for memorial purposes is declared a misdemeanor.

The county shall apply to the United States for a suitable headstone, and shall have the headstone set in place. 

Acknowledgements of written instruments executed by members of the 

Armed Forces of the United States; validity.

Administration of estates of members of the Armed Forces listed as missing in action, captured by the enemy, etc. Termination of conservatorship.

Aid is provided for dependents of persons serving with the Armed Forces.

Privilege from arrest.-Persons in the Armed Forces shall be privileged from arrest (except for treason, felony, or breach of the peace) during such military service.

Provision for withholding from sale during the war and for 1 year thereafter, State tax lands, in which persons in military service owned any estate or enforceable legal interest at the time title vested in the State.
