Absentee registration and voting_

Equal privileges on common carriers, places of amusement, hotels, etc., shall be accorded persons in uniform.

Notary services.-Persons authorized to administer oaths may not charge a veteran or his legal representative more than 15 cents for administering an oath, or giving any official certificate in connection with a claim for pension, bounty, or back pay. Such persons may not charge the widow, guardian to a minor child, or other legal representative of a veteran more than 25 cents for administering an oath.

Federal or State law.

The fee of a judge of probate is limited to 15 cents for a certificate of guardianship, administration, or of the death of a pensioner, or the widow, children, or other legal representatives of a pensioner, for the purpose of procuring the payment of any installment of pension.

Résumé Transcripts under seal of any records of the offices of secretary of state, adjutant general, judges of probate, county clerks, and justices of peace pertaining to pensions, insurance payments, or annuities are to be furnished without charge to veterans, their widows or other dependents. 

Recording of death notices. Local registrars shall record death of members of the Armed Forces.

Recording of discharge papers.-Discharge papers are recorded and indexed free of charge by county clerks.

Service officers, etc.-Duties of the Michigan Veterans' Trust Fund 

Board of Trustees and the veteran county committees to assist veterans and their dependents.

High school and county normal school students who have enlisted or been inducted into military service are exempt from requirement as to course in civics in order to graduate. 

Professional and occupational licenses. No license held by a person to engage in the profession or occupation shall expire while such licensee is in the Armed Forces of the United States. 

Board of law examiners, in its discretion, may allow an applicant who has been called into or volunteers for the Armed Forces, to be examined for the law before completion of all the educational requirements.

Veterans of World War II are not to be disqualified or placed at a disadvantage because of such service, upon seeking license for trade, occupation or profession.

Vocational school at Pine Lake shall be continued as a State institution for rehabilitation of veterans and disabled adults. 

Aid is provided for children of veterans of any war or war condition who as a result of wartime service are disabled or killed in action. Free tuition in State institutions of secondary or college grade. 

Employment security benefits are provided for veterans Custodian of the Michigan veterans' headquarters in the capitol shall be a veteran of World War I or World War II.

Motor vehicles.-Exemption from registration of motor vehicle owned by person in active service in time of war or other emergency, if not operated on public highways except by the owner while on furlough or leave of absence.

Suspension of fiduciary powers of the fiduciary while he is on, or awaiting, active duty during time of war or other major military engagement with a foreign nation.

Definition of veteran for purposes of laws administered by the Commissioner of Veterans' Affairs. 

MINNESOTA-Continued Résumé 

Service officers, etc.-Duties of the commissioner of veterans' affairs in assisting veterans having claims against the United States for pension, bounty, or backpay arising out of or by reason of any war or any Federal military or naval service.

The adjutant general shall act as the agent for veterans having claims against the United States for pensions, bounty, or backpay, arising out of or by reason of war or Federal service, and prosecute such claims without charge.

The veterans' service officer shall aid all veterans in securing benefits provided by law on account of military service. 

Veterans referral centers may be established in cities of the second class to assist veterans.

Veteran now defined as any person honorably discharged from the Armed Forces of the United States, naming various conflicts, and on or after Dec. 7, 1941, and until final cessation of hostilities as determined by proper State authorities does not require service in a combat area, excludes persons enlisted for active duty training of 6 months or less. Vietnam is specifically included.

State college board may contract with Federal Government for instruction, etc. of veterans.

Watchmakers may be exempt from examination upon application for certificate within 1 year after discharge from military service.

Provision for tuition for veterans where the benefits from United States have been exhausted, up to $250.

University of Minnesota.-Refund of tuition to students who enter military service before termination of school year for which tuition was paid.

Professional licenses and certificates of registration may be renewed within 6 months after discharge from military service.

Architects, engineers, and surveyors.-Veterans of World War I and II 

shall be given credit for experience or education gained in the armed services as meets standards set by the State board.

Benefits under the Unemployment Compensation Act_ 

Fireman.-Service with the Armed Forces shall be included in period of service for purpose of retirement. Leaves of absence for State and municipal employees in the Armed Forces of the United States; reemployment without loss of seniority rights, insurance benefits, etc., upon termination of military service.

Veteran redefined to include after World War I, any person who served on active duty for other than training purposes after Dec. 7, 1941, and was honorably discharged or was honorably discharged for disability incurred while serving on active duty, who enlisted from Minnesota or has been a resident of the State of Minnesota, and the political subdivision to which application is made for 5 years immediately preceding the application.

Includes persons who served with allied forces. Veterans perference of 5 points for nondisabled, added to score on competitive examination, and veteran's name placed ahead of nonveterans with the same score.

A disabled veteran is given 10 points in open competitive examination if the augmented rating gives him a passing grade and he is able to perform the duties of the position with reasonable efficiency, and his name is placed at the head of the eligible list. § 197.09.

Same preference is extended to widows of deceased veterans and to the spouse of a disabled veteran who because of such disability is unable to qualify. Statute states that a person so preferred shall not be precluded from holding a position in the classified service because of age or disability if it does not render him incompetent to perform the duties of the position. The foregoing applies to the State classified service only.

Compulsory retirement of an officer or employee in the classified service of the State upon reaching age 70, notwithstanding provisions of the veterans preference laws, became effective Jan. 2, 1968. § 268.08. § 69.47.

Résumé Absolute preference to war veterans, widows, and spouses of disabled veterans in appointment and employment in public departments of the State and political subdivisions thereof, including municipalities and school districts. No distinction made between disabled and nondisabled veterans. Different procedures established where the governmental unit has not established civil service or merit system and where it does not.

No removal of a veteran except for incompetence or misconduct after hearing.

Forces in time of war.

MINNESOTA-Continued 

Résumé Use of soldiers' welfare fund to aid veterans and their dependents in securing compensation, hospitalization, or other such benefits and to maintain such persons in an emergency.

Veterans referral centers may be established in any city of the second class. 

Village aid to ex-servicemen.

War veterans' fund is created to provide veterans of World War II and the Korean conflict with the necessities of life and such other needs as may assist in their rehabilitation. Expenditures to be made by the commissioner of veterans' affairs with a maximum of $250 per veteran. 

Benefits limited to within five years after discharge. 

Gift tax.-Gifts to or for the use of veterans' organizations are exempt from gift tax.

Income tax.-Abatement of State income tax imposed for year of death in service in World War II or in service after Dec. 31, 1949, and for taxes unpaid at time of his death.

Exemption of first $3,000 received as compensation for personal services in the Armed Forces of the United States or of any of the United Nations. 

Exemption of mustering out payments_

Bonus payments (World War II and Korea) shall be exempt from taxation.

Motor vehicles.-Chauffeurs' license may be renewed without charge or physical examination for veterans serving subsequent to Dec. 7, 1941. 

Vehicles of persons in active military service are exempt from registration; vehicles furnished by Federal Government and equipped for disabled veterans are exempt from registration tax. 

Peddler's license.-No license fee or charge under State law in county of veteran's residence for a peddler's or hawker's license, solely on his own account, however a municipality may collect its customary fees.

Professional and occupational licenses. Exemption from payment of renewal fees or application for renewal during military service and for 6 months thereafter, license remains in effect during service without any action to renew.

Restoration of right of suffrage to any honorably discharged veteran of World Wars I and II who may have lost such right by reason of conviction of crime. Not applicable to anyone having an unfinished or suspended sentence.

Notary services.-Officers of posts of veterans' organizations may perform notarial services in connection with matters affecting veterans. 

No charge may be made for any such service. 

Commissioned officers of certain rank may perform notarial acts for a member of the Armed Forces or the husband or wife of such member, or for a member of the merchant marine. 

The State service commissioner and assistants are authorized to administer oaths and affirmations, to take acknowledgments, and to perform all other duties required of notaries public.

Résumé Benefit rights under the employment security law may be preserved for persons in the Armed Forces.

State veterans' affairs board and State service commissioner.-Preference shall be given to veterans as members of the State veterans' affairs board and in the appointment of the State service commissioner and assistant commissioners. 

Highway Safety Patrol.-Preference shall be given to former patrolmen who were called into military service. Patrolmen are not exempt from military service. 

Employees who entered the Armed Forces after Sept. 16, 1940, shall be credited for such military service, under the public employees retirement system.

All public officers and employees are entitled to leave of absence, without loss of pay, etc., when called into military service, and must be reinstated to former position upon termination of such service. 

Any person reinstated shall not be removed, without cause, for 1 year. 

Public officers and employees' retirement system gives credit for service rendered in the Armed Forces.

Restrictions on retired persons being employed or receiving pay for services by the State are not applicable to war veterans receiving disability compensation.

National Guard retirement.-Credit for service with the Armed Forces of the United States.

and the Korean conflict. [Exemption expired July 1, 1956.]

War Veterans Memorial Commission.-Created and powers prescribed. 

Gettysburg Memorial Commission.-Created to select monument for 

Gettysburg Battlefield to honor Confederate dead.

Classifies certain military personnel stationed in the State as residents to eliminate payment by them of nonresident tuition rates in State colleges.

Members of armed services over 18 years of age given legal capacity to execute a will.

Pensions to Confederate veterans, their unremarried widows, and servants.

Income tax.-Exemption of amounts received through war risk insurance or other benefits or relief for injured or disabled members of the military or naval forces of the United States. 

Occupation tax.-Persons with 50 percent disability are exempt from privilege tax on certain occupations. [While this provision does not refer specifically to veterans, it has previously been carried in this report and is therefore continued.]

Property tax.-Homesteads owned by persons inducted into the military service are, with certain limitations and exceptions, exempt from property tax. 

Confederate soldiers' home is exempt from property tax- 

Confederate soldiers and widows.-County assessors are required to make an annual enumeration of all ex-Confederate soldiers and their widows residing in the counties.

Motor vehicle operator's license.-Not required of a person while operating vehicle of the Army, Navy, or Marine Corps. 

Poll tax.-Members of the Armed Forces and veterans are exempt from payment of poll tax; procedures.

Roadhouses.-Posts, etc., of veterans' organizations may operate roadhouses, night clubs, etc., without payment of privilege tax. 

Motor carriers. Reduced rates or free transportation may be given to inmates of soldiers' homes, to disabled soldiers and sailors, and those returning from discharge.

Résumé Acknowledgments of written instruments executed by members of the

Habeas corpus.-A person in the Army or Navy may not be discharged under writ of habeas corpus.

Jury service.-A person on active duty in the Armed Forces is ineligible to serve as a juror.

States or any State. 

Public record certification.-Adjutant general shall keep records of all servicemen, and shall furnish, free of charge, a certificate of war service to any veteran or his lineal descendant. 

Counties to maintain list of names of residents discharged from the Armed Forces; and to furnish 1 certified copy of discharge to a veteran upon request.

Custodians of records are required to furnish free of charge, to veterans or their dependents, copies of any public record for use in connection with claims against the United States.

Recording of discharges.--A county recorder of deeds (including the recorder of deeds of the city of St. Louis) is required to record discharges free of charge.

Certain counties are to record and furnish discharges upon request_ Recorders of deeds of all counties and of the city of St. Louis shall without fee record discharges and furnish certified copies when required to perfect a claim upon the Government of the United States or the 

State of Missouri.

 Taxation.

Veterans' organizations-- 

Director of Veterans' Affairs and his subordinates are required to aid and assist veterans and their dependents.

Income tax.-Donations to veterans' posts or organizations may be deducted from gross income.

Forces is not taxable and need not be included in his tax return.

Bonus 

MONTANA 

Résumé 

World War II.-An "honorarium," amounting to $10 per month for domestic service, and $15 per month for foreign service, was pro vided for World War II veterans. Surviving relatives of servicemen who died in line of duty, were authorized to be paid $500. Application deadline for veterans was Dec. 31, 1959; surviving relatives may file up to Jan. 1, 1961.

Korean conflict.-An "honorarium," in the same amounts as for World 

War II veterans is provided for Korean veterans and their survivors. 

Application deadline for both the veterans and their survivors is Jan. 1, 1961. 

World War I.-An "honorarium," in the same amounts as for World

War II and Korean veterans is provided for World War I veterans and their survivors, but does not provide for any lump sum payment to surviving relatives of servicemen who died in line of duty. 

Application deadline for both veterans and their survivors is Jan. 1, 1968. 

Burial.-County commissioners to pay $150 for burial expenses of veteran; burials not to be in cemeteries for pauper dead; not applicable to inmates of State soldiers' home and nonresidents. 

Extends burial allowance to any female resident of the Montana Veterans' Home. This extends the benefit to the wives or widows of veterans who are residents of the Montana Veterans' Home. 

Headstones. The county clerk shall apply to the U.S. Government for suitable headstones to be placed at graves of veterans. 

Headstones.-The fee paid by the clerk of the board of county commissioners for cartage and setting of the headstone for a veteran's grave, as provided by the U.S. Government, was increased from $10 to $20.

Acknowledgments of written instruments executed by members of the 

Armed Forces; validity.

Evidence of death or other status of person missing in action. 

Emergency license.-Any engineer, whose Montana license expired while he was serving in Armed Forces of United States, can renew Citation

Drivers' licenses.-A valid Montana driver's license, held by any person entering the Armed Forces of the United States, continues in full force as long as such service continues and for a period of 30 days thereafter unless suspended or revoked for cause. 

Owners of livestock.-Privileges and immunities granted under the Grass

Conservation Act to be preserved in the case of owners serving in the Armed Forces as provided in the Soldiers' and Sailors' Civil Relief Act.

Jury duty.-Persons receiving compensation from the Government for disabilities received while serving with the Armed Forces during 

World War I. 

Absentee registration and voting by electors in U.S. service.-Application for Absentee Ballot. Statute was amended twice in 1959 session but provision as application for absentee ballot by any elector in 

U.S. service is unchanged. 

Public record certification. Certified copies of record required to prove eligibility for veterans' benefits to be furnished free of charge. 

Recording of discharge.-Honorable discharges shall be recorded by the county clerk free of charge.

Service officers, etc.-Veterans' welfare commission with statewide service to veterans and their families. Section relating to veterans' welfare fund and bonds of director and commission employees repealed by chapter 80, session laws of 1961 and chapter 177, session laws of 1965. 

Some amendments added three sections which allows commission to enter into contracts with the Federal Government for reimbursement for work performed for Federal Government and also to accept Federal funds and empower commission to render services necessary to ascertain qualifications from veteran training under pt. VIII, Public Law 346/78C.

State University.-Free tuition and fee in various units, including the law and medical departments. Not applicable to veterans who qualify under provisions of the Servicemen's Readjustment Act. 

License fees for professions and occupations not required of person in military service and for 6 months thereafter.

"On-farm training account."-School districts may set up a separate account to receive and disburse funds for operation programs for veterans under Federal laws.

State university.-Children of veterans who served in World War II or the Korean conflict, and who died as a result of such service, may attend without payment of tuition and other fees; conditions. 1965 amendments define periods of eligibility and allow extension of eligibility for time served in Armed Forces of the United States. 

Police department.-Age restriction for appointment of policemen in municipalities not applicable to veterans war service credit upon retirement. 

Veterans' burial supervisor.-Preference shall be given to honorably discharged soldier, sailor, or marine in appointment of supervisor.

Public employment.-Preference shall be given to veterans, their unremarried widows, and dependents in appointments in public departments and on public works of the State and local governments. 

In examinations disabled veterans, their wives, etc., shall have 10 percent added to earned ratings on examinations; and 5 percent to all others. 

Public works contracts.-Labor preference to honorably discharged soldiers, sailors, and marines required under Federal statutes to be preserved in State contracts.

Veterans' welfare commission.Members of commission to have had service in the military forces.

Reemployment-restoration of officers and employees of State and local units to former positions without loss of seniority or insurance benefits, upon termination of military service. 

Retirement systems.-State highway patrol, provision for payment into fund during military.

Hospital benefits.

Meeting places, memorial buildings.

Restoration of State and civil employees to former positions without loss of seniority or insurance benefits, upon termination of military service.

Schools are not obligated, under §§ 77-601, to rehire teachers or instructors until beginning of next semester or quarter. 

Uniform Guardianship Act.-Provides for the appointment of a guardian of an incompetent veteran or his minor child, to receive certain benefits on behalf of such ward.

Commitment of an incompetent veteran to a Federal hospital under the Uniform Guardianship Act.

Building and loan associations and banks may make loans to qualified veterans, restrictions on extension or renewal of loans not to apply to such veterans. 

Minority disability of otherwise eligible veteran and his minor spouse removed in certain cases.

Provision for the construction and furnishing of a building as an adjunct to the State capitol building to be known as the Montana Veterans and Pioneers Memorial Building. 

Establishes the Veterans and Pioneers Memorial Building as a perpetual memorial to war veterans and pioneers of Montana and states that the State of Montana can receive gifts in behalf of this memorial. 

Provides for appropriation and budgeting of veteran memorial moneys for purposes of constructing, maintaining and furnishing the veterans quarters of the Veterans Pioneers Memorial Building. 

Veterans Welfare Commission to establish statewide service for veterans to cooperate with State and Federal agencies having to do with affairs of veterans and their families, and to administer the veterans welfare fund.

To accept Federal funds to carry out purposes within the scope of its activities including necessary services in making surveys as to qualifications of establishments for on-the-job training to veterans. 

Amusements tax.-Veterans' organizations are exempt from certain exhibition license fees.

Fishing and hunting licenses.-Members of the Armed Forces and their spouses on duty within the State considered to be residents after 30 days for purposes of obtaining licenses; waiver of 30-day requirement in time of war.

Income Tax.-Deferment of collection of tax upon income of persons in the military service.

Résumé Income Tax.

-Payments under the Korean bonus law and the World 

War I and World War II veterans' bonus law exempted from paying 

State income tax.

Professional and occupational fees.—Suspension from payment of professional and occupational license fees by persons in the military service. 

Drivers license.-Members of the Armed Forces of United States, while operating a vehicle leased or owned by United States on official business do not need Montana drivers license.

Poll tax.-Exemption of certain World War I veterans- 

Property tax. Exemption of certain property (a) of veterans' organizations, (b) in the possession of legal guardians of incompetent veterans of World War I, or minor children of such veterans, where such property is funds or derived from funds received from the United States as pensions, compensation, insurance, adjusted compensation or gratuity, as property of the United States while held by the guardian, but not after title passes to the veteran or minor in his or her own right on removal of legal disability.

All taxes on property owned by persons in military or naval service shall be held in abeyance until expiration of 1 year after cessation of hostilities or discharge.

Alcoholic beverages licenses.-Provision for issuance of liquor licenses to posts of nationally chartered veterans' organizations, which had been in existence for at least 5 years prior to Jan. 1, 1949.

Provision for issuance of beer license to any post of nationally chartered veterans' organization which had been in existence for at least 5 years prior to Jan. 1, 1949.

Provides for issuance of a 24-hour special permit for a nationally chartered veterans' organization to sell beer to members and guests on the post premises.

Meeting places, etc.-Quarters to be provided in the Montana Veterans and Pioneers Memorial Building for use of veterans' organizations. Citation

NEBRASKA-Continued 

Résumé Uniform veterans' guardianship.-Provides for the appointment of a guardian of an incompetent veteran or the minor child of a veteran, to receive certain benefits on behalf of such ward. 

Soldiers' and Sailors' Homes.-Admission of veterans, their wives or widows and mothers.

Liquor sales.-No license shall be issued for the sale of alcoholic liquor within 150 feet of a veterans' home.

Commitment of mentally ill veterans to a hospital or facility operated by the Veterans' Administration. 

Building and loan associations are authorized to make loans pursuant to provisions of the Servicemen's Readjustment Act. 

Removal of minority disability for veterans and their spouses for benefits under the Servicemen's Readjustment Act.

Statutes, monuments, or other memorials commemorating services of soldiers and sailors may be erected and located upon public lands or within public buildings within a county, township, city, or village. 

Veterans' Day (November 11) is a legal holiday; and the Governor is required to issue a proclamation annually calling for its proper observance. 

Decoration Day (May 30).-Desecration of the day is deemed a misdemeanor, punishable by a fine up to $100 and/or imprisonment up to 30 days.

Death certificates of residents of Nebraska who died while in the military service may be recorded with the department of health. 

Nebraska veterans' aid fund.-Administration, disbursement, etc., for aid of needy veterans and their dependents.

County service committees are to furnish aid to indigent veterans, wives, widows, or minor children.

Domestic insurance companies may invest funds in bonds, etc., guaran teed under provisions of the Servicemen's Readjustment Act. 

Soldiers', sailors', and marines' relief fund: Tax shall be levied by counties for creation of fund.

NEVADA 

Revised Statutes, through 1967

Résumé Burial.-Any Grand Army post or United Spanish War Veterans camp which furnishes burial expenses to indigent ex-soldiers within the State shall be entitled to an allowance by the county of from $25 to $40.

Acknowledgements of written instruments executed by members of the 

Armed Forces of the United States; validity.

Administration of estates of members of the Armed Forces listed as missing in action, captured by the enemy, etc.; termination of conservatorship.

Evidence of death or other status of persons missing in action.. 

Motor vehicle operators' or chauffeurs' licenses held by persons in the military service shall not expire until termination of such service.

Right of suffrage shall be enjoyed by all persons (otherwise eligible) in the military or naval service of the United States; poll tax not required.

Absentee registration and voting by members of the Armed Forces, their spouses and dependents.

Notary services.-Public officers may not make charges for administering oaths or certifying or acknowledging any papers for U.S. pensioners in matters relating to pensions or compensation. 

Public-record certification.-Free copies of public records shall be provided for wards of the United States under the Uniform Guardianship Act.

Free copies of public records shall be furnished when required by the Veterans' Administration in determining eligibility of any person to receive benefits thereunder. 

Veterans' Service Commissioner is now known as Nevada Commissioner for Veterans' Affairs.

NEVADA-Continued Résumé Housing authority may make projects available to war veterans. 

Low-cost housing.-Preference shall be given to families of disabled veterans by housing authorities in rentals and tenant admissions. 

Low-rental housing.-Housing authorities may acquire property for construction of projects to be leased to servicemen, veterans, and their families at rentals not to exceed $50 per month during periods of acute housing shortage.

Commitment of an incompetent veteran to a Federal hospital, under the Uniform Guardianship Act.

Conveyance of certain real property in the city of Reno, to the United States as site for erection of a veterans' hospital. 

A committed patient may be transferred to a VA hospital or facility if eligible for treatment therein.

Insane persons subject to regulations upon commitment to VA Hospital.. 

Removal of minority, disability of veterans, and their minor spouses, eligible for benefits under the Servicemen's Readjustment Act.

Powers and duties of the veterans' service commissioner. 

Amusements tax.-Veterans' organizations are exempt from fee for license for tent shows and circuses for not over 2 weeks in any calendar year.

Loans.-Loans to veterans under the Veterans' Business Loan Act, etc., shall be exempt from taxation.

Motor vehicles.-Free registration of vehicles furnished resident disabled veterans under Federal law.

Refund of registration fee to person entering the military or naval services of the United States.

Peddler's license.-Special license may be issued to veterans (without cost) to hawk, peddle, and vend goods, wares, or merchandise, or solicit trade within the State. Such license is restricted to the county wherein licensee resides.

Professional and occupational licenses.-Certain disabled veterans are entitled to free license to act as insurance brokers or agents. 

Professional and occupational licenses.-Certain disabled veterans are entitled to free licenses to act as real estate brokers or salesmen. 

Property tax.-Annual listing of veterans' tax exemptions by the collector of taxes or assessor of each taxing district. 

Property owned and used by a corporation organized to provide instruction in agricultural pursuits for crippled soldiers and sailors is exempt.

Property used in the work of veterans' organizations existing and established on June 18, 1936, is exempt from tax, if the legal or beneficial ownership of the property is in one or more of said organizations, or posts, or affiliated associations.

Veterans and their widows are entitled to a reduction of $50 on their tax bill on real and personal property or both or through cancellation thereof if such tax is less than $50. 

Real and personal property used by an organization composed entirely of veterans shall be exempt from tax.

Dwelling house of a veteran who has a service-connected disability from paraplegia; or from total blindness; or from amputation of both arms or both legs; etc., is exempt from tax.

Free registration of automobile furnished by the Federal Government to veterans entitled to compensation for the loss, or loss of use, of one or both legs.

Industrial commissioner is empowered to make agreements for Federal reimbursement to the State, of certain expenses in connection with veterans' training pursuant to the Servicemen's Readjustment Act. 

Insurance agent or broker.-Renewal of insurance broker's license after termination of military service.

Temporary licensing, without examination, of designee of insurance agent or broker serving in the Armed Forces (effective until July 1, 1964).

Ophthalmic dispensing.-Issuance of certificate, without examination, to practice ophthalmology. Application must be filed within 6 months after discharge from military service (applicable to persons in the 

Armed Forces on July 1, 1947). (This provision is probably obsolete.)

Real estate brokers and salesmen.-Renewal of licenses, without examination, after termination of military service.

City service officers.-Appointment of veterans as deputies or assistants. 

Civil Service. Disqualification of a veteran because of disability or age is prohibited.

Preference shall be given to veterans in appointments and promotions in the civil service of the State and all of the civil divisions thereof. 

Preference shall be given to veterans in appointment, promotion, and retention in any branch of the civil service, State, county, or municipal. 

Firemen.-Leave of absence shall be granted to volunteer firemen, for military service.

War service shall be credited for the purpose of qualifying as a volunteer fireman.

Notaries public.-May be reappointed after expiration of military service in the Armed Forces of the United States.

Résumé  - General functions, powers, and duties of the State division of veterans' affairs and of local veterans' service agencies.

Health, medical and rehabilitation services are provided by State 

Veterans' Service Agency. 

Assistance, relief, and medical care of veterans and their families in their homes, administered through veteran organizations and public welfare districts.

Motor vehicles specially designed for the use of veteran amputees and furnished by the Federal Government are exempt from registration fees.

Reduced rates on common carriers may be allowed for veterans attending educational institutions pursuant to the Servicemen's Readjustment Act.

Income tax.-Abatement of income tax of a member of the Armed 

Forces upon death in active service. 

Exemption of military compensation received during any month during any part of which taxpayer served in combat zone, or was hospitalized as a result of such service.

Annual pension of $500 is provided for blind veterans and their widows_ Bureau of War Records.-Provision for the preservation and safekeeping of records and relics in the capitol. 

Horse and harness races.-Soldiers and sailors in uniform may be admitted to horse and harness races at half price. 

Recreations centers.--Towns adjoining military camps are authorized to establish and maintain recreation centers. 

For income tax purposes, persons in the Armed Forces who are not domiciled in the State, even if maintaining a permanent abode and spending in the aggregate more than 183 days of taxable year within the State, shall not be deemed a resident.

Returns for taxable years during which a taxpayer was in the military service of the United States, or hospitalized outside the State, shall be made to the tax commissioner within 6 months after termination of service or return to the State.

Veterans' organizations...

Motor vehicles.-Registration without payment of fees is allowed on motor vehicles owned and operated by disabled veterans who obtained such vehicles from the Federal Government under laws (Public 

Law 663, 79th Cong.; Public Law 798, 81st Cong.; and Public Law 

187, 82d Cong.), which provide for furnishing specially designed automobiles to veteran amputees.

Refund of fees to owner who enters the Armed Forces, upon surrender of number plates or tags.

Professional licenses.-Refund of fees paid for license by a private detective and investigator when inducted into the Armed Forces. 

Real estate brokers.-Refund of fees for licenses issued to persons inducted into the military service; conditions.

Property tax.-Exemption of real property purchased with money collected by popular subscription in partial recognition of extraordinary services rendered by discharged veterans who sustained total or partial permanent disability if owned by the person who sustained such injuries, or by his wife or widow, or dependent father or mother. 

Maximum exemption $5,000.

Exemption of real property purchased with the proceeds of a pension, bonus, or insurance granted by the United States or the State of New York for military or naval services, if owned by the person who rendered the service, his wife or widow, dependent father or mother, or by his minor children. Such property is not exempt from local school taxes. Maximum exemption, $5,000.

Lot and dwelling of any disabled veteran who has received pecuniary assistance from the United States for a house with special facilities, etc., is exempt up to $10,000, in addition to other property exemptions.

Monument corporations exempt from real property taxes and execution. 

Real property of veterans' organizations is exempt from tax. 

Peddlers' license.-Veterans or their widows are exempt from payment of fee for county license.

Unauthorized wearing of military uniform is declared a misdemeanor.- 

Amusements.-Veterans organizations are authorized to conduct bingo games.

Recording of discharges.-Official discharges from the Armed Forces shall be recorded in a permanent book provided for such purpose.

Income tax.-Insurance or compensation received from any of the

Allied countries because of injuries received during World War I and

Forces or the merchant marine shall be exempt from payment of taxes and fees during such service.

Résumé Property tax.-Exemption of property of the American Legion and of other veterans' and patriotic organizations and associations. 

No penalty, fee, or interest shall be assessed or collected on delinquent ad valorem taxes assessed against the real or personal property of members of the Armed Forces during the period of World 

War II. 

Motor vehicle.--Registration fee of only $1 shall be charged for vehicle given by Federal Government to disabled veteran of World War II, Korean conflict or other veteran entitled to receive under 38 U.S.C. 

252 (now § 1901, et seq.); such vehicle exempt from property tax. 

Veterans are exempt under certain circumstances from the prohibition on wearing the uniform of the Armed Forces of the United States. 

Amusements license.-Certain restrictions not to apply to veterans' organizations and posts holding fairs and tobacco festivals; conditions. 

Corporate rights, etc.-North Carolina Department of Veterans Affairsthe department commander or head of each recognized veterans' organization shall be an ex officio member of the State board of veterans affairs. 

Insignia. The unauthorized wearing or use of the insignia of the American Legion is declared a misdemeanor punishable by a fine up to $50 and/or imprisonment up to 30 days.

Public record certification.—The office of vital statistics is required to furnish, without charge, to officers of certain veterans' organizations, copies of vital statistical records. 

Tax exemptions.--Property of the American Legion and other veterans' organizations are exempt from property tax. 

Billiard and pool tables, and bowling alleys.-American Legion posts and other veterans' organizations are exempt from license tax to operate.

NORTH DAKOTA Century Code 

Résumé [World War I.-Provisions of law for payment of bonus to veterans of 

World War I have long since expired.] 

[World War II.-Provisions for payments to World War II veterans are likewise obsolete. The time limit for filing applications was July 1, 1954; reexamination of claims was permitted up to Sept. 30, 1955.] 

Korean conflict.-Provisions for payment to Korean conflict veterans are likewise obsolete. The time limit for filing applications was Feb. 

15, 1960.

Death certificates.-State registrar of vital statistics is directed, upon request, to issue and file death certificates of veterans.

Grave registration.-The adjutant general is required to register and compile records of persons who rendered military service, service with the Merchant Marine, and who assisted in administration of the 

Selective Service Act, during World War II and the Korean conflict. 

Record of deceased soldiers, sailors, and marines. The adjutant is required to compile and keep record of burial places within the State of persons who served in Armed Forces in time of war. Undertakers shall indicate in death certificate if deceased was a veteran.

Acknowledgments of written instruments executed by members of the 

Armed Forces of the United States; validity.

Evidence of death or other status of persons missing in action. 

Hunting, trapping, or fishing.-Resident license may be issued to nonresident members of the Armed Forces.

Absentee voting by electors in the Armed Forces- Public record certification.-Certified copy of any public record required by a veteran, guardian, dependent, or representative, in connection with applicatlon for Federal or State benefits, shall be furnished without charge.

Copies of public records required to determine eligibility for veterans benefits shall be furnished free of charge. 

Recording of discharges.—Recordation of discharge papers without payment of fee. One certified copy may be furnished a veteran or his next of kin, free of charge.

Transportation, motor vehicle privileges, etc. 

Veterans' organizations.

NORTH DAKOTA-Continued Résumé 

Income.--Military compensation is exempt from State income tax. 

Motor vehicles.-Disabled veterans owning specially constructed automobile furnished by the United States shall be exempt from payment of registration fee, except $1 for special license plates; special emblems furnished.

Servicemen serving outside State more than 1 year may relicense motor vehicle owned by him without paying fees for intervening years upon proof vehicle was not in use in any year in which it was not licensed.

Barbers.-A person, holding a license as a barber, who enter military service, may obtain restoration of his license without payment of fee. 

Property tax.-Land used as a public park or monument ground belonging to a military organization shall be exempt from tax. 

Fixtures, buildings, and improvements up to a net assessed valuation of $10,000 for paraplegic disabled veterans, and $4,000, or in the alternative personal property up to an assessed valuation of $4,000, used and owned as a homestead by another disabled veteran with a disability of greater than 50 percent or his widow if the veteran is deceased, provided the veteran and his wife or his unremarried widow do not earn more than $3,000 net during the calendar year. 

Amputees are exempt from payment of registration fee, use and sales tax on automobiles; but are required to pay $1 for special license plates.

Insignia.-Unauthorized wearing or use of badge, button, or insignia of a veterans' organization is declared a misdemeanor, punishable by a fine of from $10 to $100. 

Meeting places.-Use of armories authorized.

Parades. Veterans' organizations are authorized to parade in public with firearms.

Tax exemptions.-Land used as a public park or monument ground of a military organization shall be exempt from tax. 

Uniforms.-Members of veterans' associations are exempt from the prohibition on the wearing of military uniforms.

Veterans' organizations_ - OHIO Continued Résumé Peddler's license.-Veterans of the Civil War, Spanish-American War, and World War I, are required to pay a nominal fee only. 

Property tax.-Exemption of real estate held or occupied by soldiers' memorial associations, monumental building associations, etc. Such property is not relieved from levy of special assessments. 

Funds raised and set apart for the purpose of building monuments to the soldiers of the State, and monuments and memorial buildings are exempt from tax.

Group insurance.-Provision is made for policies of group life insurance covering members of veterans' organizations. 

Insignia.—Unauthorized wearing or use of insignia, badges, etc., of certain veterans' organizations or the display thereof on motor vehicles or otherwise is punishable by fine not to exceed $20 and/or imprisonment for not more than 30 days. 

Unauthorized wearing or use of insignia, badges, etc., of United

Veterans of the Republic is declared a misdemeanor, punishable by a fine up to $100 and/or imprisonment up to 6 months. 

Meeting places, etc.- -Memorial buildings established and maintained by counties, townships, and municipalities shall provide, with or without cost, suitable and convenient headquarters for nationally organized veterans' organizations chartered by the Congress of the 

United States.

Powers of certain counties to erect memorial structures with rooms suitable for a meeting place for the Grand Army of the Republic, Women's Relief Corps, Sons of Veterans, or any other organization for the benefit of Civil War veterans or their descendants. 

Suitable rooms, including heat, light, and janitor service, shall be provided in State armories for various veterans' organizations, unless a State, county, or municipal memorial building has been provided.

Township memorial buildings are to be open and free to and for the use of veterans' and allied organizations as meeting places.

Defines war veterans as such honorably discharged persons as (a) served in the military or naval forces of the United States at any time during the period from Apr. 6, 1917, to Nov. 11, 1918, or (b) served at any time during the period of Dec. 7, 1941, to Sept. 2, 1945, or (c) served at any time from June 27, 1950, and Jan. 31, 1955, or (d) served for a period of 180 days or more (unless discharged from active duty for a service-connected disability) on or after Aug. 5, 1964, during the period of time in which the United States participated in a war compaign or battle, but excluding any person who shall have served on active duty for training only, unless discharged from active duty for a service-connected disability.

Canadian forces.-Veterans who served with the Armed Forces of Canada during World War II, who were residents of the State for 1 year prior to such service, shall be entitled to veterans' benefits. 

Court costs shall not be required in any proceeding to appoint a guardian to approve or authorize the ward to enter the Armed Forces.

Veterans' Day (Nov. 11) is declared to be a legal holiday.

Oklahoma veterans home.-Establishment on site of the Oklahoma Confederate Home and the Southern Oklahoma Hospital, under management and control of the war veterans' commission. 

Oklahoma State war veterans' home facilities.-Established at Sulphur, Ardmore, and Norman, for special treatment and care of veterans.

Petty cash funds are created at certain war veterans' home facilities--- Property formerly part of Union Soldiers' Home transferred to the control, supervision, and management of the State board of public affairs for the benefit of the State.

State board of public affairs may execute agreement with Veterans of 

Foreign Wars for use of pasture lands of the Whitaker State Orphans 

Home at Pryor.

Wives and widows of veterans may be eligible for admission to State 

War Veterans Facilities. 

Persons in the Armed Forces and their families are not required to be domiciled on a homestead during time of war or national emergency; no bar to asserting claim.

Cigarettes and tobacco products sold to veterans' hospitals for distribution or sale to disabled veterans shall be exempt from tax. 

Taft State hospital for the insane.-Establishment of a separate ward for the care and treatment of veterans.

Soldiers' Memorial Hospital, and Oklahoma Central State Hospital.- 

Maintenance and use of funds. 

Oklahoma State Veterans' Hospital revolving fund created; petty cash fund to be included.

Dry cleaning. Inspection fees, etc. not applicable to veterans.

Gift tax.-Gifts made to veterans' organizations are exempt from tax…. 

Income tax.-Military compensation not to exceed $1,500 in any 1 year is exempt from tax; extension of time for filing returns. 

Inheritance tax.-Exemption of war-risk insurance, adjusted service compensation, proceeds from any Federal bonus, pension, etc.

Motor vehicles.--Disabled veterans who have been awarded vehicles by the U.S. Government entitled to license and identification plates for vehicle and replacements for sum of $1.

Peddler's license.-Needy or disabled veterans may engage in hawking or peddling or in giving illustrated lectures, etc., without payment of license tax.

Embalmers and funeral directors are exempt from payment of license renewal fees during active military service.

Property tax.-Property up to $200 in value (in addition to the $100 allowance) is exempt from tax for all honorably discharged veterans or their widows. 

Insignia. Unauthorized wearing or use of badges, pins, or insignia of veterans' organizations is a misdemeanor punishable by a fine of not less than $25 or more than $100 and/or imprisonment up to 30 days. 

Meeting places, etc.—County Commissioners may lease, rent, or donate any room in the courthouse for use of veterans' organizations. 

Rooms in the historical society building may be set aside for the use of veterans' organizations.

Industrial Selling Act provisions shall not apply to veterans' organizations. 

Privileges, duties, etc.-Assistance in the selection of members of the war veterans' commission; copy of annual audit is required to be filed with Secretary of State.

Tax exemptions.-Gifts to veterans' organizations shall be exempt from

Gift tax.-Gifts to veterans' organizations are allowed as deductions in computing net gifts and net income. 

Income tax. Exemption of compensation in an amount not to exceed $3,000 received for active service in the Armed Forces of the United States, in addition to personal exemption; no withholding of income tax from military compensation.

Exclude from gross income (for income tax purposes) sums taken from retired serviceman's pay to provide an annuity for surviving dependents.

Bonus given to World War II veterans, exempt from all taxes including income and inheritance tax. 

Inheritance Tax.-Exempts from inheritance taxation the value of all benefits not exceeding the sum of $20,000 of pension or retirement benefits established by Federal Government; applies to estates of decedents dying on or after Sept. 13, 1967.

Professional and occupational licenses.-Renewal of professional and trade licenses within 60 days following military service, without payment of the current license fees.

Provision for an additional $500 exemption on homesteads of unremarried widows of veterans of Civil or Spanish-American Wars.

Burial grounds.-Counties may appropriate money to purchase soldiers' burial plots and to provide for the perpetual care thereof.

Authorizes the military department and veterans' organizations to assist in obtaining cemetery maps and filing information relating to unregistered burials and other records. Requires that each grave located be designated by a separate and distinct uniform marker. 

All burial permits shall contain the military record of decedent, which record shall be filed with the military department of the State. 

Headstones and markers.-State allowance may be made for erecting headstone provided by the U.S. Government for the purpose of marking the grave of a veteran.

Acknowledgments of written instruments executed by members of the 

Armed Forces of the United States; validity.

Administration of estates of members of the Armed Forces listed as missing in action, captured by the enemy, etc. Termination of conservatorship.

Divorce actions.-Corroborative evidence in divorce actions may be furnished by depositions in lieu of personal appearance when one of the parties is in military service.

Notarial acts.-Oaths, etc., may be administered by military officers; no fee. 

Powers of attorney granted by persons serving in the Armed Forces of the United States, effect of death.

State guard.-Members of the National Guard of the State are not exempt from military service under laws of the United States. 

Absentee voting by members of the Armed Forces.. 

Public-record certification.-Certified copies of recorded certificates of discharge shall be furnished free of charge when requested for the purpose of presenting or prosecuting claims for compensation or pension.

Business tax.-A disabled veteran of any war, or any peacetime soldier, sailor, or marine who was disabled in regular service, owning less than $2,500 of property and doing business with a capital stock of not exceeding $2,500, shall be exempt from payment of State privilege taxes.

VERMONT-Continued 

Résumé Payment of tax on service income may be deferred for a period of 6 months after termination of military service; no interest shall accrue.

Poll tax. Exemption for honorably discharged veterans, their wives and widows, provided the veteran served in the Civil War, the Spanish-American War, the Boxer Rebellion, the Philippine Insur rection, World War I, World War II or the Korean conflict. For the veteran and wife he must be in receipt of compensation or pension. 

There is no requirement that a widow be in receipt of a benefit. 

Property tax.-Exemption of real property of veterans of the Civil and 

Spanish-American Wars and of service between Apr. 21, 1898, and

July 4, 1902, or their widows; and exemption on home property to the extent of $6,000 of appraisal value of veteran of any war, his spouse, widow or child, upon submission of VA statement showing payment of 50 percent wartime disability compensation, wartime death compensation, or wartime DIC, or that pension is payable. 

Insignia.—Unauthorized wearing or use of a badge, button, or insignia of a veterans' organization is punishable by imprisonment for not more than 30 days or fine up to $50 or both.

Meeting places.-Lease of State property in the city of Montpelier to national veterans' organizations for a nominal fee. 

Towns may appropriate funds for rental of rooms for use of veterans' organizations.

Tax exemption.-Real property of veterans' organizations exempt from tax.

State veterans' board within the office of the adjutant general.. VIRGINIA

§ 51-1.

Acknowledgements of legal instruments executed by members of the Armed Forces of the United States; validity. The 1964 amendment inserted any State National Guard is federally recognized and added every acknowledgment executed prior to Jan. 1, 1964, in substantial compliance with the provisions of this section is hereby validated, ratified, and confirmed, notwithstanding any error or omission with respect to any address, grade, or rank. 

Administration of estates of members of the Armed Forces listed as missing in action, captured by the enemy, etc. Termination of conservatorship.

A soldier in actual military service or a mariner or a seaman being at sea, may dispose of his personal estate as he might have done prior to statutory regulations. 

Bail bonds forfeited because of absence in military service: proceedings in favor of defendant.

Deeds, etc., executed by persons in the military service under defective powers of attorney may be validated. 

Depositions are not to be read against incompetent ex-servicemen for whom trustees have been appointed except under certain circumstances.

Jury service.-Persons in the military service are exempt from jury duty.

Motor vehicles.-Operator's licenses are extended for the period of military service outside the State and for 6 months thereafter but any such extension granted under the provisions of this section shall not exceed 2 years from the date of expiration shown on individual operator's license.

Mentally ill persons—transfer to Federal hospitals.—Veterans found to be mentally ill may be ordered to a veterans' hospital or other suitable place for treatment.

Life insurance.-Violation of condition in policy relating to naval and military service in time of war is declared to be an exception as to incontestability after 3 years.

Under the Land Settlement Act, veterans are to be provided useful employment and rural homes. 

Removal of minority disability of veterans eligible for benefits under the Servicemen's Readjustment Act. 

Restrictions on the making, renewing, or extending of real estate loans shall not apply to loans guaranteed under the Servicemen's Readjustment Act.

Use of pensions of war veterans by State institutions is prohibited, except that a limited amount may be used in the case of a veteran confined in a charitable institution of the State, with no dependents.

WYOMING-Continued Résumé 

Motor vehicles.-Exemption from current registration of foreign vehicles owned and operated by members of the Armed Forces. 

Operator's license is not required of members of the military forces operating vehicles of the military service. 

Exemption of war veterans from county registration fee in certain cases.

Property to the amount of $2,000 of its assessed value, of veterans, including nurses, and their widows during widowhood, shall be exempt property tax. Stipulations are set forth as to certain conditions for claimants. Total tax benefit is not to exceed $800, except does not apply to widows during widowhood or service-connected disabled veterans. § 31-72. § 31-254. § 31-16(e). § 39-7(5th). Citation 

Term "veteran" is defined for purposes of tax exemptions; statement of §§ 39-8, 39-9. veterans exemptions allowed by counties throughout the State.
